The differences of phase transitions in estimation and clustering are enumerated in Table
3
. Basically, strongly consistent estimation is always possible as long as
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gtrsim d^{1/2}n^{1/4}
. In contrast, weakly efficient clustering is possible only when
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gtrsim n^{1/2}+d^{1/2}n^{1/4}
, and consistent clustering is possible only when
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gtrsim d^{1/2}n^{1/4}
and meanwhile
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gg n^{1/2}
. Note that the gap between estimation and clustering is present only under the low-dimensional setting
n\gtrsim d^{2}
. The gap vanishes under the high-dimensional setting
d^{2}\gg n
, in which case the signal strength condition
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gtrsim d^{1/2}n^{1/4}
already implies
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gg n^{1/2}
.

Sample size

Signal strength

Consistent estimation

Weakly efficient clustering

Consistent clustering

d^{2}\lesssim n

d^{1/2}{n^{1/4}}\lesssim\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\lesssim n^{1/2}

Possible

Impossible

Impossible

n^{1/2}\lesssim\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\lesssim n^{1/2}

Possible

Possible

Impossible

\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gg n^{1/2}

Possible

Possible

Possible

d^{2}\gg n

\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gtrsim d^{1/2}{n^{1/4}}

Possible

Possible

Possible

Table 3
:
The differences of phase transitions in estimation and clustering for two-component symmetric LrMM under the regime of strong signal strength
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}\gtrsim d^{1/2}n^{1/4}
. Here
d^{2}\gg n
is referred to as the high-dimensional setting, and
d^{2}\lesssim n
as the low-dimensional setting.

We collect these facts to convince that, at least for the two-component symmetric LrMM (
16
), clustering is intrinsically more challenging than estimation. The same phenomenon also arises in GMM. See, e.g.,
Wu and Zhou 2019
.

6.2
Example where estimation is more challenging

While, generally, clustering is recognized as being more challenging than estimation, there are examples where clustering is easier than estimation. Similarly as in Section
5
, consider the two-component LrMM with population center matrices
\mathbf{M}_{1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}
so that

\sigma_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{M}_{1})\geq C_{1}\Big(1+\frac{d^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}+\frac{d^{1/2}}{n^{1/4}}\Big)\quad{\rm and}\quad\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{M}_{2})\leq C_{1}^{-1}\cdot\frac{d^{1/2}}{n^{1/2}}

where
C_{1}>0
is a large constant and, for simplicity, we assume
\kappa_{0},\alpha,r_{1},r_{2}=O(1)
. Observe that

\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}}\cdot\frac{\sigma_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{M}_{1})}{\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{M}_{2})}\geq\begin{cases}C_{1}^{2}n^{1/4},&\textrm{ if }n\leq d^{2};\\
C_{1}^{2}(n/d)^{1/2},&\textrm{ if }n>d^{2};\end{cases}\quad\to\infty,\quad{\rm as}\ n\to\infty

Moreover,

\Delta:=\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\|_{\rm F}\gtrsim C_{1}\Big(1+\frac{d^{1/2}}{n^{1/4}}\Big)\to\infty

if the constant
C_{1}>0
diverges to infinity. Therefore, by Theorem
6
, if
C_{1}\to\infty
, our Algorithm
3
consistently cluster all observations.

However, consistent estimation of the population center matrices is more challenging. Even if all the latent labels are correctly identified, estimation of
\mathbf{M}_{2}
is still impossible because of its weak signal strength. Indeed, the low-rank approximation to

\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast}):=\frac{1}{n_{2}^{\ast}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=2\right)\mathbf{X}_{i}

achieves the error rate (in expectation)
O(d^{1/2}n^{-1/2})
and the relative error rate (in expectation) diverges to infinity as
C_{1}\to\infty
. Similarly, the trivial estimate by a zero matrix attains the relative error rate
1
that never vanishes as
n\to\infty
. Consequently, a strongly consistent estimate of
\mathbf{M}_{2}
becomes impossible.

7
Discussions

7.1
Estimation of
r_{\mathbf{U}}
,
r_{\mathbf{V}}
,
K
and
r_{k}
’s

Our tensor-based spectral initialization method requires an input of ranks
r_{\mathbf{U}}
,
r_{\mathbf{V}}
and the number of clusters
K
, which are usually unknown in practice. Under the decomposition (
9
), they constitute the Tucker ranks of tensor
{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}
. Several approaches are available to estimate the Tucker ranks for tensor PCA model. One typical approach
(
Jing et al. 2021
;
Cai et al. 2022
)
is to check the scree plots
(
Cattell 1966
)
of
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
, respectively. Under a suitable signal strength condition as in Theorem
2
, the scree plots of
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
shall serve a reliable estimate of
r_{\mathbf{U}}
and
r_{\mathbf{V}}
, respectively. However, we note that it is statistically more efficient to estimate
K
by, instead, taking the scree plot of
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top})
, where
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}
are obtained in step 1 of Algorithm
2
. This additional spectral projection promotes further noise reduction as in Algorithm
2
. After obtaining
r_{\mathbf{U}}
,
r_{\mathbf{V}}
and
K
, an initial clustering
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)}
can be attained by apply Algorithm
2
. Similarly, we then estimate the rank
r_{k}
by the scree plot of the sample average of matrix observations whose initial labels are
k
. It provides a valid estimate as long as the initial clustering is sufficiently good.
The aforementioned approach works nicely in
real-world data applications. See Section
8
for more details.

7.2
Matrix observation with categorical entries

Oftentimes, the matrix observations consist of categorical entries. For instance, the Malaria parasite gene networks (see Section
8.2.3
) have binary entries (Bernoulli distribution); the 4D-scanning transmission electron microscopy
(
Han et al. 2022b
)
produces count-type entries (Poisson distribution). Our algorithms are still applicable and deliver appealing performance on, e.g., Malaria parasite gene networks dataset. Unfortunately, our theory can not directly cover those cases, although the noise are still sub-Gaussian. Without loss of generality, let us consider multi-layer binary networks and assume
\mathbf{X}_{i}
has Bernoulli entries. Then the entries of
\mathbf{X}_{i}
have an equal variance only when they have the same expectation, reducing the network to a trivial Erdős-Rényi graph. Nevertheless, equal noise variance is crucial to establish Theorem
2
. Moreover, the techniques for proving Theorem
1
are likely sub-optimal since the sub-Gaussian constant
\sigma_{\textsf{sg}}
is usually not sharp enough to characterize a Bernoulli random variable. We leave this to future works.

8
Numerical Experiments and Real Data Applications

8.1
Numerical Experiments

This section presents the empirical performance of lr-Lloyd’s algorithm (Algorithm
1
) and its relaxed variant under weak SNR (Algorithm
3
) referred to as the rlr-Lloyd’s algorithm. Specifically, we focus on the algorithmic convergence and final clustering error.

In the first simulation setting
S1
, we fix the dimension
d_{1}=d_{2}=50
and sample size
n=200
. The latent labels
s_{i}^{\ast}
are generated i.i.d. from the model (
2
) with equal mixing probabilities, i.e.,
\pi_{k}=1/K
. All the presented results in
S1
are based on the average of
30
independent trials. We test the convergence of Algorithm
1
under both Gaussian (
S1-1
) and Bernoulli (
S1-2
) noise.

In
S1-1
, we set
K=2
,
r_{1}=r_{2}=2
and standard Gaussian noise. The population center matrices
\mathbf{M}_{1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}
are generated in the following manner. For each
k=1,2
, we independently generate a
d_{1}\times d_{2}
matrix with i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries and extract its top-
2
left and right singular vectors as
\mathbf{U}_{k}
and
\mathbf{V}_{k}
, respectively. The singular values are manually set as
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{k}=\text{diag}\{1.2\lambda,\lambda\}
for some fix
\lambda>0
. Then the population center matrices are constructed as
\mathbf{M}_{k}=\mathbf{U}_{k}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{k}\mathbf{V}_{k}^{\top}
. Our experiment tries four levels of signal strength
\lambda\in\{1.9,2.1,2.3,2.5\}
. For each
\lambda
, the population center matrices are generated as above and the separation strength is recorded. The corresponding separation strength are
\Delta\in\{4.22,4.66,5.11,5.45\}
. At each level of signal strength, the observations
\{\mathbf{X}_{i}:i=1,\cdots,200\}
are independently drawn from (
4
) with the obtained center matrices
\mathbf{M}_{1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}
. Here we focus on the convergence behavior of Lloyd’s iterations of Algorithm
1
, and thus a warm initial clustering
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)}
is provided before hand. The same initial clustering is used for all simulations and the initial clustering error is
n^{-1}h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=0.45
, i.e., slightly better than a random guess. Convergence of Algorithm
1
under four levels of signal strength (or, correspondingly, separation strength) is displayed in the left plot of Figure
1
. The decreasing of log of clustering error is linear in first few iterations, as expected by our Theorem
1
. The algorithm converges fast and the final clustering error is reflected by the separation strength
\Delta
. It is worth pointing out that Figure
1
(a) also shows that Algorithm
1
converges faster when
\Delta
becomes larger. While this cannot be directly concluded from Theorem
1
, it can be easily verified by checking the proof.

In
S1-2
, we test the effectiveness of Algorithm
1
under non-Gaussian and non-i.i.d. noise. In particular, we consider the mixture multi-layer stochastic block model (MMSBM) introduced in
Jing et al. 2021

5
5

5

We emphasize that our Theorem
1
is not directly applicable to MMSBM due to non-i.i.d. noise.
. We set the number of clusters
K=3
. For each
k=1,2,3
, the
k
-th SBM is associated with a connection probability matrix
\mathbf{B}_{k}\in[0,1]^{K\times K}
and a membership matrix
\mathbf{Z}_{k}\in\{0,1\}^{d\times K}
, which are set as
\mathbf{B}_{k}:=\bar{p}_{k}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{K}+\bar{p}_{k}/2\cdot(\mathbf{1}_{K}\mathbf{1}_{K}^{\top}-\mathbf{I}_{K})
with
\bar{p}_{k}=\bar{p}\cdot k/K
and
\mathbf{Z}_{k}(i,:)=\mathbf{e}_{s^{*}_{i}}
, respectively. Thus each SBM has three cluster of nodes and the population center matrices are
\mathbf{M}_{k}=\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathbf{B}_{k}\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{\top}\in[0,1]^{d\times d}
. Conditioned on the latent label
\mathbf{s}_{i}^{\ast}
, the
i
-th observation
\mathbf{X}_{i}
is sampled from SBM(
\mathbf{Z}_{s_{i}^{\ast}},\mathbf{B}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}
), namely,
\mathbf{X}_{i}(j_{1},j_{2})\sim\text{Bernoulli}(\mathbf{M}_{s^{*}_{i}}(j_{1},j_{2}))
and
\mathbf{X}_{i}(j_{2},j_{1})=\mathbf{X}_{i}(j_{1},j_{2})
for
1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}\leq d
. Note that
\mathbf{X}_{i}
is symmetric because the network is undirected. We manually set the diagonal entries of
\mathbf{X}_{i}
to zeros so that no self-loop is allowed in the observed network. Clearly, the entry-wise variances of
\mathbf{X}_{i}
are not necessarily equal. Under the above MMSBM, the signal strength and separation strength are characterized by sparsity level
\bar{p}
. Four sparsity levels
\bar{p}\in\{0.05,0.08,0.10,0.15\}
are studied so that the corresponding separation strength are
\Delta\in\{0.75,1.19,1.46,2.15\}
. Similarly, a fixed good initial clustering
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)}
is used for all simulations and the initial clustering error is
n^{-1}h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=0.3
. Convergence behavior of Algorithm
1
is displayed in the right plot of Figure
1
. Still, Lloyd’s iterations converges fast and the final clustering error is decided by the separation strength
\Delta
.

(a)

Simulation
S1-1
: Log of clustering error (
K=2
) with
\Delta
varying under Gaussian noise.

(b)

Simulation

) with

(Convergence behavior of Algorithm

) Log of clustering error with

varying under two scenarios: LrMM with Gaussian noise and MMSBM with Bernoulli noise.

In the second simulation setting
S2
, we aim to compare the final clustering error of vanilla Lloyd’s algorithm and our low-rank Lloyd’s algorithm. The dimensions are varied at two cases
d_{1}=d_{2}\in\{50,100\}
, sample size is set as
n\in\{100,200\}
, number of clusters
K=2
and ranks
r_{1}=r_{2}=3
. The latent labels are generated as in
S1
. For each
d_{1}
and
n
, the simulation is repeated for 100 times and their average clustering error rate is reported.

In
S2-1
, the population center matrices
\mathbf{M}_{1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}
are constructed such that they share identical singular spaces. More exactly, we extract singular vectors
\mathbf{U}_{1}
,
\mathbf{V}_{1}
and singular value matrix
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{1}
as is done in
S1-1
. Then the population center matrices are set as
\mathbf{M}_{1}=\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}=\mathbf{U}_{1}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{1}+\text{diag}\{\Delta/3,\Delta/3,\Delta/3\})\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}
. Here the signal strength is fixed at
\lambda=10
and the separation parameter is chosen from
\Delta\in\{1,5,10\}
. The final clustering error and its standard error by four methods are reported in the upper half of Table
4
.
Noted that the initialization of “vec-Lloyd” in
Lu and Zhou 2016
is attained by spectral clustering on
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
.
We observe that the clustering errors of four methods all decrease as
\Delta
increases. However, lr-Lloyd initialized by Algorithm
2
achieves a much smaller clustering error compared with other methods. This is due to the fact that our proposed tensor-based spectral initialization is capable to capture the low-rank signal whereas both spectral clustering and naive K-means on
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
ignores the low-rank structure in the other two modes of
{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}
. As a result, all the other three methods perform almost the same under current setting. Lastly, the bold-font column in Table
4
confirms Theorem
1
in that the clustering error achieved by TS-init initialized lr-Lloyd algorithm is only determined by
\Delta
regardless of the dimension
d_{1},d_{2}
or the sample size
n
.

In
S2-2
, the singular vectors of
\mathbf{M}_{1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}
are generated exactly the same as in
S1-1
. The singular values of
\mathbf{M}_{1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}
are set as
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{1}={\rm diag}(1.2\lambda,1.1\lambda,\lambda)
and
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{2}={\rm diag}(0.36,0.33,0.30)
, respectively. Then
\sigma_{\textsf{\tiny min}}(\mathbf{M}_{1})=\lambda
and
\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{M}_{2})=0.36
. Here
\lambda
is varied at
\{1.9,2.2,2.5\}
for the case
d_{1}=d_{2}=50
and
\{2.7,3.0,3.3\}
for the case
d_{1}=d_{2}=100
. Consequently, the signal strength of
\mathbf{M}_{2}
is much smaller than
\mathbf{M}_{1}
that corresponds to the weak SNR setting in Section
5
, and we test the performance of the relaxed lr-Lloyd’s algorithm (Algorithm
3
). The results are reported in the lower half of Table
4
. Clearly, rlr-Lloyd’s algorithm outperforms the vanilla Lloyd’s algorithm (i.e., the vectorized version). In certain cases, the vanilla Lloyd’s algorithm merely beats a random guess whereas the rlr-Lloyd’s algorithm almost achieves zero clustering error. We also observe that rlr-Lloyd’s algorithm still performs nicely if initialized by K-means on
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
.

8.2
Real Data Applications

We now demonstrate the merits of our proposed low-rank Lloyd’s (lr-Lloyd) algorithm on several real-world datasets and compare with existing methods.

8.2.1
BHL dataset

The BHL (brain, heart and lung ) dataset
6
6

6

The dataset is publicly available at
https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sites/GDSbrowser?acc=GDS1083
.
, which had been analyzed in
Mai et al. 2021
, consists of
d_{1}=1124
gene expression profiles of
n=27
brain, heart, or lung tissues. Each tissue is measured repeatedly for
d_{2}=4
times and hence the
i
th sample can be constructed as
\mathbf{X}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{1124\times 4}
for
i=1,\cdots,27
. Our aim is to correctly identify those
\mathbf{X}_{i}
’s belonging to the same type of tissue, i.e.,
K=3
. We apply Algorithm
1
together with an initial clustering
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)}
obtained by Algorithm
2
with
r_{\mathbf{U}}=r_{\mathbf{V}}=1
. These ranks are chosen based on the scree plots of
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
. The final clustering error attained by lr-Lloyd’s algorithm is
n^{-1}\cdot h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=0.03704
.
As shown in Table
5
, our lr-Lloyd’s algorithm performs the best among all the competitors
7
7

7

Note that all results except lr-Lloyd are directly borrowed from
Mai et al. 2021
, which use
\mathbf{X}_{i}
’s after dimension reduction to a size of either
20\times 4
or
30\times 4
, and we only report the better one here.
that are reported in
Mai et al. 2021
.

lr-Lloyd

DEEM

K-means

SKM

DTC

TBM

EM

AFPF

Clustering error

3.70

7.41

11.11

11.11

18.52

11.11

11.11

11.11

Table 5
:
Clustering error on BHL dataset. SKM: sparse K-means
(
Witten and Tibshirani 2010
)
; DTC: dynamic tensor clustering
(
Sun and Li 2019
)
; TBM: tensor block model (TBM)
(
Wang and Zeng 2019
)
; EM: standard EM implemented in
Mai et al. 2021
; AFPF: adaptive pairwise fusion penalized clustering
(
Guo et al. 2010
)
.

The improvement can be attributed to two reasons. First, DEEM in
Mai et al. 2021
is designed based on EM algorithm targeted at Gaussian probability distribution, and hence they need to first perform multiple Kolmogorov-Smirnov tests to drop the columns not following Gaussian distribution, which might lead to potential information loss. In sharp contrast, their procedure is not necessary for our method, as the low-rank Lloyd’s algorithm allows for sub-Gaussian noise. Secondly, our algorithm is more suitable for the specific structure of the data. Particularly, the population center matrices are expected to be rank-one as the columns of
\mathbf{X}_{i}
represent repeated measurements for the same sample. However, such planted structure is under-exploited in
Mai et al. 2021
and others.

8.2.2
EEG dataset

The EEG dataset
8
8

8

The dataset is publicly available at
https://archive.ics.uci.edu/ml/datasets/EEG+Database
.
has been extensively studied by various statistical models
(
Li et al. 2010
;
Zhou and Li 2014
;
Hu et al. 2020
;
Huang et al. 2022
)
. The goal is to inspect EEG correlations of genetic predisposition to alcoholism.
The data contains measurements which were sampled at
d_{1}=256
Hz for
1
second, from
d_{2}=64
electrodes placed on each scalp of
n=122
subjects. Each subject, either being
alcoholic
or not, completed 120 trials under different stimuli. More detailed description of the dataset can be found in
Zhang et al. 1995
. For our application, we average all the trials for each subject under single stimulus condition (S1) and two matched stimuli condition (S2), respectively, and construct the data tensor as
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(S_{1})}\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times 64\times 122}
(or
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(S_{2})}\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times 64\times 122}
) after standardization. Thus each subject is associated with a
256\times 64
matrix, and we aim to cluster these subjects into
K=2
groups, corresponding to alcholic group and control group. We apply rlr-Lloyd’s algorithm (Algorithm
3
) with
r_{\mathbf{U}}=r_{\mathbf{V}}=3
and
r_{1}=2,r_{2}=1
. Here
r_{\mathbf{U}}
and
r_{\mathbf{V}}
are selected by the scree plot of
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
, and
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
are tuned by interpreting the final outcomes. The clustering error of our method and competitors are shown in Table
6
. It is worth pointing out that our task of clustering is generally more challenging than classification, which has been investigated on the EEG dataset
(
Li et al. 2010
;
Zhou and Li 2014
;
Hu et al. 2020
;
Huang et al. 2022
)
. Those classification approaches often achieve lower
classification
error rates. As a faithful comparison, our rlr-Lloyd’s algorithm enjoys a superior performance to its competitors in terms of
clustering
error rate and time complexity.

Surprisingly, we note that the original lr-Lloyd’s algorithm (Algorithm
1
+ Algorithm
2
) would not deliver a satisfactory result on this dataset. It can be partially explained by Figure
2
, which displays the average of all trials under S2 for two groups. It is readily seen that the average matrix of control group is comparatively close to pure noise, and hence the relaxed version of lr-Lloyd’s algorithm can work reasonably well in this scenario.

Clustering error of EEG dataset under S1 and S2. Note that the methods vec-Lloyd and SKM
(

)
refer to directly applying Lloyd’s algorithm and sparse K-means on vectorized data, i.e., on rows of
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(S_{1})})
or
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(S_{2})})
, whereas DTC
(
Sun and Li 2019
)
and TBM
(
Wang and Zeng 2019
)
are both tensor-based clustering methods.

8.2.3
Malaria parasite genes networks dataset

We then consider the
var
genes networks of the human malaria
parasite
Plasmodium falciparum
constructed by

Larremore et al. 2013
via mapping
n=9
highly variable regions
(HVRs) to a multi-layer network.

(a)

HVR1.

(b)

HVR2.

(c)

HVR3.

(d)

HVR4.

(e)

HVR5.

(f)

HVR6.

(g)

HVR7.

(h)

HVR8.

(i)

HVR9.

Figure 3
:
Malaria parasite genes networks dataset: 9 highly variable regions (HVRs) represented by their adjacency matrices
(
Jing et al. 2021
)

Following the practice in
Jing et al. 2021
, we focus on
d_{1}=d_{2}=212
common nodes appearing on all
9
layers and obtain a multi-layer network adjacency tensor
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}\in\{0,1\}^{212\times 212\times 9}
with each layer being the associated adjacency matrix. Unfortunately, the method in
Larremore et al. 2013
needs to discard
3
out of
9
HVRs due to their extreme sparse structures, referring to region
\{2,3,4\}
in Figure
3
. This later had been remedied by the tensor-decomposition-based method TWIST in
Jing et al. 2021
. In term of clustering all layers, we expect our algorithm would have a comparable performance in contrast with the results in
Jing et al. 2021
. Specifically,
Jing et al. 2021
obtain a hierarchical structure with
6
clusters of all layers by repeatedly clustering the embedding vectors. Following their practice, by setting
(r_{\mathbf{U}},r_{\mathbf{V}},K)=(15,15,6)
, we apply Algorithm
2
on
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}
, and find that the
9
HVRs fall in to the following clusters:
\{1\},\{2,3,4,5\},\{6\},\{7\},\{8\},\{9\}
. The result is exactly the same as that in
Jing et al. 2021
but our method avoid repeated clustering. We remark that our tensor-based spectral initialization already produces a good initial clustering on this dataset, and thus further low-rank Lloyd’s iterations seem unnecessary. In sharp contrast, it would lead to unsatisfactory result if we directly apply K-means with
K=6
on the embedding matrix obtained by TWIST. This further demonstrates the validity and flexibility of our proposed lr-Lloyd’s algorithm.

8.2.4
UN comtrade trade flow networks dataset

In the last example, we consider the international commodity trade flow data in
2019
in terms of countries/regions and different types of commodities, collected by
Lyu et al. 2021
from
UN comtrade Database
9
9

9

The dataset is publicly available at
https://comtrade.un.org
.
. Following the data processing procedure in
Lyu et al. 2021
, we pick out top
d_{1}=d_{2}=48
countries/regions ranked by exports and obtain a weighted adjacency tensor
\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{48\times 48\times 97}
, where
n=97
layers represent different categories of commodities
10
10

10

The categories are based on 2-digit HS code in
https://www.foreign-trade.com/reference/hscode.htm
.
. The entry
\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3})
indicates the amount of exports from country
i_{1}
to country
i_{2}
in terms of commodity type
i_{3}
. To have a comparable magnitude across different entries, our data tensor is obtained after transformation
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}=\log(\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}+1)
. We emphasize that in
Lyu et al. 2021
the edges of
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}
have to be further converted to binary under their framework, which might cause undesirable information loss. We apply Algorithm
1
that is initialized by Algorithm
2
with parameters
(r_{\mathbf{U}},r_{\mathbf{V}},K)=(3,3,2)
and
(r_{1},r_{2})=(2,2)
. These choices produce most interpretable result as summarized in Table
7
. It is intriguing to notice that cluster 1 mainly consists of products of low durability including animal & vegetable products and part of foodstuffs, whereas cluster 2 contains most industrial products that might indicate a trend of global trading. These findings are consistent with
Lyu et al. 2021
.

Appendix A
Proofs of Main Theorems

Throughout the proofs, we use
c,C,C^{\prime}
to represent generic absolute constants, whose actual values may vary in different formulas.

A.1
Proof of Theorem
1

Step 1: Notations and Good Initialization

We need to introduce some notations to simplify the presentation of our proof. Recall the
individual signal strength
is defined as

\lambda=\min_{k\in[K]}\sigma_{\textsf{\tiny min}}(\mathbf{M}_{k})

Note in our setting, we simply have
\lambda\gtrsim\kappa_{0}^{-1}r^{-1/2}\max_{a\neq b}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{b}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\kappa_{0}^{-1}r^{-1/2}\Delta
.

Define the frobenius error with respect to the true label
\mathbf{s}^{\ast}
:

\ell(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

as well as the corresponding hamming loss:

h(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}\neq s_{i}^{\ast}\right)

A simple relation is that
h(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\Delta^{-2}\cdot\ell(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
due to the fact

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}\neq s_{i}^{\ast}\right)\Delta^{2}.

Note that, by definition
\ell_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{(0)}}-\mathbf{M}_{\pi(s_{i}^{\ast})}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

for some permutation
\pi
, we can always relabel our
\mathbf{M}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{M}_{K}
to
\mathbf{M}_{\pi(1)},\cdots,\mathbf{M}_{\pi(K)}
after initialization. Therefore, without loss of generality we can assume
\pi=\text{Id}
and hence
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=\ell_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
. As a result of condition (
10
), we also have

\displaystyle h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\frac{\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2}}=o\left(\frac{\alpha n}{\kappa_{0}^{2}K}\right)

(20)

Note that (
10
) can be equivalently expressed as
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\tau
for some
\tau=o\left(\kappa_{0}^{-2}{\alpha n\Delta^{2}}/{K}\right)
and hence
\Delta^{2}\gg\kappa_{0}^{2}K\tau/(\alpha n)
.

Step 2: Iterative Convergence

We then analyze the convergence property of low-rank Lloyd algorithm. Without loss of generality, given the labelling
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)}
at the
(t-1)
-th iteration, we investigate the behavior of
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)}
, i.e., after one iteration of Lloyd algorithm.

To simplify the presentation, the subsequent analysis is conducted on the following events, where
C>0
is some absolute constant.

{\cal Q}_{1}=\bigcap_{a\in[K]}\left\{\left\|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)}}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{\ast}_{a}}}\right\}

{\cal Q}_{2}=\bigcap_{I\in[n]}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{|I|}}\sum_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\leq C\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{n}\right)\right\}

{\cal Q}_{3}=\bigcap_{i\in[n],a\in[K]}\left\{\left\{\left\|\frac{\sum_{j\neq i}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{j}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)}}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d+\log n}{n^{\ast}_{a}}}\right\}\bigcap\left\{\left\|\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{d+\log n}\right\}\right\}

The following lemma dictates that
{\cal Q}_{1}\cap{\cal Q}_{2}\cap{\cal Q}_{3}
occurs with high probability.

Lemma 3
.

There exists some absolute constants
C_{0},c_{0}>0
such that if
d\geq C_{0}\log K
, then

\mathbb{P}\left(Q_{1}^{c}\cup Q_{2}^{c}\cup Q_{3}^{c}\right)\leq\exp(-c_{0}d)

Our goal is to establish the following relation between two successive iterations:

\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s})\leq 2n\cdot\exp\left\{-\big(1-o(1)\big)\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8}\right\}+\frac{1}{2}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s})

(21)

and prove that it holds with high probability for all positive integer
t
.

Suppose for iteration
t-1
,
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
satisfies (
10
) and
h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
satisfies (
42
), which will be validated via induction in the last step. By the definition of
\widehat{\bm{s}}^{(t)}
, we have for each
i\in[n]
:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

Rearranging terms above, we obtain

\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}}\right\rangle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+{\cal R}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)};\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)}\right)

(22)

where

{\cal R}\left(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)}\right):=\frac{1}{2}\left[\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right]

Without loss of generality, suppose
\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a
for some
a\in[K]
. Set
\delta=o(1)
that is to be determined later. The following fact is obvious.

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{a}\right\rangle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+{\cal R}(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1-\delta}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle+{\cal R}(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1-\delta}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{4}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\mathbb{I}\left({\cal R}(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\geq\frac{\delta}{4}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

By the definition of
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
, we have

\displaystyle\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1-\delta}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{4}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\mathbb{I}\left({\cal R}(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\geq\frac{\delta}{4}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=:\xi_{\textsf{err}}+\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})+\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

where we define

\displaystyle\xi_{\textsf{err}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1-\delta}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{4}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left({\cal R}(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\geq\frac{\delta}{4}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

It suffices to bound
\xi_{\textsf{err}},\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
and
\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
, respectively.

Step 2.1: Bounding
\xi_{\textsf{err}}
.

Let us begin with
\mathbb{E}\xi_{\textsf{err}}
. By definition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\xi_{\textsf{err}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1-\delta}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}\right)

Note that
\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle
is normal distribution with mean zero and variance
\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\|_{\rm F}^{2}
. The standard concentration inequality of normal random variable yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{1-\delta}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\delta)^{2}}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\xi_{\textsf{err}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\delta)^{2}}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right).

Assume
n\gg K
,
\Delta^{2}\gg\log K
and let
\delta
converge to
0
as slow as possible, we can get

\mathbb{E}\xi_{\textsf{err}}\leq n\cdot\exp\left\{-\big(1-o(1)\big)\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8}\right\}

By Markov inequality,

\mathbb{P}\left(\xi_{\textsf{err}}\geq\exp(\Delta)\mathbb{E}\xi_{\textsf{err}}\right)\leq\exp(-\Delta)

We conclude that, with probability at least
1-\exp(-\Delta)
,

\xi_{\textsf{err}}\leq\exp(\Delta)\mathbb{E}\xi_{\textsf{err}}\leq n\cdot\exp\left\{-\big(1-o(1)\big)\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8}\right\}

Step 2.2: Bounding
\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

By definition,

\displaystyle\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\beta_{1,1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})+\beta_{1,2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

Without loss of generality, we only prove the upper bound of the second term
\beta_{1,2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
. Notice that the labels
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)}
depend on all the noise matrices
\{\mathbf{E}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, thus
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}
is dependent on
\mathbf{E}_{i}
. Delicate treatment is necessary to establish a sharp upper bound for
\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
.

Recall the definition that
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}
is computed by the best rank-
r_{a}
approximation of
\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)}):=(n_{a}^{(t-1)})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)\mathbf{X}_{i}
with
n_{a}^{(t-1)}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)
. Denote
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{(t)}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{(t)}
the left and right singular vectors of
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}
. Then we have
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{(t)}(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{(t)})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{(t)}(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{(t)})^{\top}
.
For notation simplicity, we now drop the superscript
(t)
in
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{(t)}
,
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{(t)}
and write
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a},\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}
instead.

Now write

\displaystyle\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{X}}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}+\mathbf{E}_{i})}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)}}\right)\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

Recall that
n_{a}^{\ast}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)
. Denote

\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}:=(n_{a}^{\ast})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\quad{\rm and}\quad\bar{\mathbf{E}}_{a}^{(t-1)}:=(n_{a}^{(t-1)})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}

Then we can proceed as

\displaystyle\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}_{a}^{(t-1)}\right)\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\left[\mathbf{M}_{a}+\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{a})+\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+(\bar{\mathbf{E}}_{a}^{(t-1)}-\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right]\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{M}_{a}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\right)\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

where we’ve defined

\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}:=\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{a})\quad{\rm and}\quad\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}:=\bar{\mathbf{E}}_{a}^{(t-1)}-\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}

For simplicity, we denote
\Delta^{(t-1)}:=\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}
and write

\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}-\mathbf{M}_{a}=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{M}_{a}+\Delta^{(t-1)}\right)\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

(23)

Notice that since
h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
satisfies (
10
), we have that

\displaystyle n_{a}^{(t-1)}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}\neq s_{i}^{\ast}\right)

\displaystyle\geq n_{a}^{\ast}-h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\geq\frac{\alpha n}{K}-\frac{\alpha n}{8K}\geq\frac{7\alpha n}{8K}

The following lemma is useful whose proof is postponed to Section
B
.

Lemma 4
.

Suppose that
h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
satisfies (
10
). Then,

\|\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}\|\leq\frac{C_{0}K}{\alpha n}\min\left\{\kappa_{0}\lambda h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}),\frac{\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta}\right\}

for some absolute constant
C_{0}>0
, where we define

h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a,s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}\neq a,s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)

and

\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a,s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}\neq a,s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

Moreover, under event
{\cal Q}_{1}\cap{\cal Q}_{2}
, there exist absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\left\|\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}\right\|\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{dK}{\alpha n}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\|\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\|\leq C_{2}\frac{K\sqrt{(d+n)\cdot h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha n}

By Lemma
4
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta^{(t-1)}\right\|\leq c\lambda+C\left(\alpha^{-1/2}K^{1/2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\alpha^{-1}K\sqrt{\frac{h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{n}}\right)

Recall that
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{M}_{a})\geq\lambda\gtrsim\kappa_{0}^{-1}r^{-1/2}\Delta
and the condition

\Delta\gg\alpha^{-1/2}\kappa_{0}K^{1/2}r^{1/2}\left((d/n)^{1/2}+1\right)
,
we have that
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{M}_{a})\geq C\alpha^{-1/2}K^{1/2}\left((d/n)^{1/2}+1\right)
. Combining the condition that
h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=o\left(\kappa_{0}^{-2}\alpha n/K\right)
and the bound for
\Delta^{(t-1)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{M}_{a})>3\left\|\Delta^{(t-1)}\right\|

(24)

Such signal strength condition is essential to obtain a delicate representation formula for
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}-\mathbf{M}_{a}
in eq. (
23
), via the following lemma whose proof is deferred to Section
B
.

Lemma 5
.

For any rank-r matrix
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
with compact SVD
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Sigma}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
, where
\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{O}_{d_{1},r}
and
\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{O}_{d_{2},r}
and
\mathbf{\Sigma}=\text{diag}(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{r})
with
\sigma_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\sigma_{r}>0
. Let
\Delta
be an arbitrary
d_{1}\times d_{2}
perturbation matrix and
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{M}+\Delta
. Denote
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\in\mathbb{O}_{d_{1},r},\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\in\mathbb{O}_{d_{2},r}
the top-r left and right singular vectors of
\mathbf{X}
. Suppose that
\sigma_{r}>3\|\Delta\|
, then we have the following relation:

\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}&\bf 0\\
\bf 0&\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\\
\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\sum_{k\geq 1}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta)&\bf 0\\
\bf 0&\sum_{k\geq 1}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta)\\
\end{bmatrix}=\sum_{k\geq 1}{\cal S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta)

Here the
k
-th order perturbation term
{\cal S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta)
is defined as

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta):=\sum_{\mathbf{m}:m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k+1}=k}(-1)^{1+\tau(\mathbf{m})}\cdot\mathfrak{P}^{-m_{1}}\Delta^{*}\mathfrak{P}^{-m_{2}}\Delta^{*}\cdots\Delta^{*}\mathfrak{P}^{-m_{k+1}}

(25)

where
\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\cdots,m_{k+1})
contains non-negative integers,
\tau(\mathbf{m})=\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}\mathbb{I}(m_{i}>0)
and

\Delta^{*}:=\begin{bmatrix}\bf 0&\Delta\\
\Delta^{\top}&\bf 0\\
\end{bmatrix},\quad\mathfrak{P}^{-k}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bf 0&\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-k}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-k}\mathbf{U}^{\top}&\bf 0\end{array}\right)\text{ if }k\text{ is odd }\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-k}\mathbf{U}^{\top}&\bf 0\\
\bf 0&\mathbf{V}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-k}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\end{array}\right)\text{ if }k\text{ is even. }\end{array}\right.

for all
k\geq 1
. Specifically,
\mathfrak{P}^{0}=\mathfrak{P}^{\perp}
denotes the orthogonal spectral projector defined by

\mathfrak{P}^{\perp}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}&0\\
0&\mathbf{V}_{\perp}\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{\top}\end{array}\right)

By Lemma
5
and (
24
), we have the following decomposition

\displaystyle\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{M}_{a}+\Delta^{(t-1)}\right)\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{M}_{a}

\displaystyle=\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}+\mathbf{M}_{a}\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)+\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\Delta^{(t-1)}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}

so that we can re-write

\displaystyle\beta_{1,2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{32}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{32}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{32}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\Delta^{(t-1)}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{32}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(26)

It suffices to bound each term in the RHS of above equation.

Step 2.2.1: Treating the terms of
\big<\mathbf{E}_{i},\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\big)\mathbf{M}_{a}\big>

By Lemma
5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle

(27)

The RHS of (
27
) is the sum of infinite series. It turns out that delicate treatments are necessary for general
k\geq 1
. Now we write

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{32}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(28)

where
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}:={\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}\left(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\right)-{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
. We start with bounding the first term on RHS of (
A.1
). According to (
25
) in Lemma
5
, the
k
-th order perturbation term
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}
can be written as a sum of
2k\choose k
series. For notational simplicity we define for any
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}({\mathbf{B}}):=

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{V}_{a},\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{V}_{a\perp},\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{V}_{a},\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{V}_{a\perp},

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{a},\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{a\perp},\mathbf{V}_{a\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{a},\mathbf{V}_{a\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}\Big\}

By a careful inspection on (
25
) and the fact that
\mathbf{U}^{\top}_{a\perp}\mathbf{M}_{a}=0
, only terms of the form

\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}

survive in the
2k\choose k
series, where
\mathbf{U}\in\{\pm\mathbf{U}_{a},\pm\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}\}
and
\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\left\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bigcup\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}})
for
j\in[2k-1]
. Moreover, we have
\left|\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}})\}\right|=k
and
\left|\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\}\right|=k-1
. Without loss of generality for
i\in[n]
, we are going to bound the term

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

(29)

To decouple the dependence of
\mathbf{E}_{i}
and
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}
, we write

\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}=\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,i}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i}
,
where
\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,i}=(n_{a}^{\ast})^{-1}\mathbf{E}_{i}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)
and
\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i}=(n_{a}^{\ast})^{-1}\sum_{j\neq i}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{j}
. Then for any
\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}})
, we can decompose
\mathbf{W}_{j}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{j}=\mathbf{W}_{j,i}+\mathbf{W}_{j,-i}

(30)

with
\mathbf{W}_{j,i}\in{\cal M}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,i})
and
\mathbf{W}_{j,-i}\in{\cal M}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i})
. Note that on
{\cal Q}_{3}
, Lemma
3
implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,i}\right\|\lesssim(n_{a}^{\ast})^{-1}\sqrt{d+\log n},\quad\left\|\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d+\log n}{n_{a}^{*}}}

Since there are
k

\mathbf{W}_{j}
’s belonging to
\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}})
, we can substitute (
30
) back into (
29
) and obtain
2^{k}
terms. These terms can be categorized into
2
cases which will be treated separately.

1.

Term of form

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

where
\mathbf{Y}_{j}\in\left\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bigcup{\cal M}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i})
,
\left|\{j:\mathbf{Y}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\}\right|=k-1
, and
\left|\{j:\mathbf{Y}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i}})\}\right|=k
. In this case,
\mathbf{E}_{i}
is independent of
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq r_{a}\prod_{j=1}^{2k-1}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{j}\right\|^{2}\leq C^{k}\left(\frac{d+\log n}{n_{a}^{*}}\right)^{k}\frac{r_{a}}{\lambda^{2k-2}}

By general Hoeffding’s inequality, we thus obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{4k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left(\exp\left(-\frac{c\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}}{2^{8k}\left\|\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}\right)\mathbb{I}\left(\left\|\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq C^{k}\left(\frac{d+\log n}{n_{a}^{*}}\right)^{k}\frac{r_{a}}{\lambda^{2k-2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}\lambda^{2(k-1)}n_{a}^{*k}}{C^{k}r_{a}(d+\log n)^{k}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\frac{\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\cdot\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k}\right)

for some large constant
C^{\prime}>0
, where the last inequality holds due the condition
\lambda^{2}\gtrsim\alpha^{-1}K(d+\log n)/n
. Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{4k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\left(-\Delta^{2}\cdot\frac{c\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\cdot\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k}\right)

where we’ve set
\delta=o(1)
in the way that it converges to
0
sufficiently slowly compared to
\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]
. By Markov inequality, we get with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-\delta\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k/2}\left[\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]\right]^{1/2}\Delta\big)
that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{4k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\left(-\Delta^{2}\cdot\frac{c\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\cdot\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k}\right)

2.

Terms of form

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

where
\mathbf{Y}_{j}\in\left\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bigcup{\cal M}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,i})\bigcup{\cal M}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i})
,
\left|\{j:\mathbf{Y}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\}\right|=k-1
,
\left|\{j:\mathbf{Y}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,i}})\}\right|=k_{1}
,
\left|\{j:\mathbf{Y}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a,-i}})\}\right|=k_{2}
,
k_{1}+k_{2}=k
and
k_{1}\geq 1,k_{2}\geq 0
. Notice that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{r_{a}^{1/2}}{\lambda^{k-1}}\left(\frac{d+\log n}{n_{a}^{*}}\right)^{k_{2}/2}\frac{(d+\log n)^{k_{1}/2}}{(n_{a}^{\ast})^{k_{1}}}

This implies that

\displaystyle 2^{4k+6}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2^{4k+6}\left\|\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{i}\mathbf{V}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{k}r_{a}(d+\log n)^{k/2+1/2}}{\lambda^{k-1}(n_{a}^{\ast})^{k/2+k_{1}/2}}\leq C\frac{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)}{n}

where the last inequality holds as
\lambda^{2}\gtrsim\alpha^{-1}K(d+\log n)/n
and
k_{1}\geq 1
. Using the condition
\Delta^{2}\gg\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n
and
\delta\rightarrow 0
sufficiently slowly, we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{Y}_{1}\mathbf{Y}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{Y}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{4k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)=0

Collecting the above two facts, we conclude that in the
2^{k}
terms we obtained by substituting (
30
) into (
29
), one term can be bounded exponentially (case 1) and the remaining
2^{k}-1
terms vanish (case 2). Thus for (
29
), we get with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-\delta\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k/2}\left[\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]\right]^{1/2}\Delta\big)
that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{3k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\left(-\Delta^{2}\cdot\frac{c\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\cdot\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k}\right)

Recall that the
k
-th order perturbation
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}
can be written as summation of at most
2k\choose k
terms of form (
29
). Applying a union bound and a simple fact that
{2k\choose k}\leq 4^{k}
, we can conclude that with probability at least
1-4^{k}\exp\big(-\delta\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k/2}\left[\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]\right]^{1/2}\Delta\big)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot 4^{k}\exp\left(-\Delta^{2}\cdot\frac{c\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\cdot\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k}\right)

(31)

Now a union bound over all
k\geq 1
gives that with probability at least
1-\sum_{k\geq 1}4^{k}\exp\big(-\delta\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k/2}\left[\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]\right]^{1/2}\Delta\big)
, (
A.1
) holds for any
k\geq 1
. Notice that

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq 1}4^{k}\exp\left(-\delta\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k/2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\right)^{1/2}\Delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\exp\left(-2^{k}\cdot\delta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\right)^{1/2}\Delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\delta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\right)^{1/2}\Delta\right)

where the first inequality holds as
C^{\prime}
is sufficiently large (e.g.,
C^{\prime}>5
) such that
\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{k/2}\geq k\log 4
. Hence with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-\delta\left[\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]\right]^{1/2}\Delta\big)
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\left(-\Delta^{2}\cdot\frac{c\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\right)

It remains to bound the second term on RHS of (
A.1
). Notice that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=b\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{b}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{b}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=b\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{b}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\frac{2^{2k+12}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle^{2}}{\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{b}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\left\|\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|^{2}\cdot\frac{2^{2k+12}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=b\right)\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}/\left\|\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|\right\rangle^{2}}{\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{b}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}

(32)

The following lemma is needed whose proof is deferred to Section
B
.

Lemma 6
.

There exist absolute constants
c_{1},C_{1}>0
such that, for any fixed
b\in[K]
and
d_{1},d_{2}
and
r
, the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{1}d)
:

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{\Xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}},{\rm rank}(\mathbf{\Xi})\leq r\\
\|\mathbf{\Xi}\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=b\right)\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{\Xi}\right\rangle^{2}\leq C_{1}r(dr+n_{b}^{\ast})

We denote the event in Lemma
6
by
{\cal Q}_{4}
and proceed on
{\cal Q}_{4}
. By Lemma
6
and (
32
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\frac{C^{k}r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\left\|\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|^{2}

(33)

It suffices for us to have an upper bound for
\left\|\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|^{2}
. Recall that by definition
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}={\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}\left(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}-{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}
, consisting of at most
(3^{k}-1){2k\choose k}
terms in form of

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}

where
\mathbf{U}\in\{\pm\mathbf{U}_{a},\pm\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}\}
and
\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\left\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}}\right)\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}}\right)
for
j\in[2k-1]
with
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\right|=k-1
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{1}
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{2}
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{3}
and
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=k
,
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\geq 0
,
k_{1}\leq k-1
. By Lemma
4
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{W}_{j}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{dK}{\alpha n}}=:{\cal R}_{1},\quad\forall j\in\left\{l:\mathbf{W}_{l}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{W}_{j}\right\|\leq\frac{CK}{\alpha n}\cdot\min\left\{\kappa_{0}\lambda h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}),\frac{\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta}\right\}=:{\cal R}_{2},\quad\forall j\in\left\{l:\mathbf{W}_{l}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{W}_{j}\right\|\leq\frac{CK\sqrt{(d+n)\cdot h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha n}=:{\cal R}_{3},\quad\forall j\in\left\{l:\mathbf{W}_{l}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}

Using
k_{1}\leq k-1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{2k-1}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\|^{2}\leq{\lambda^{-2(k-1)}}\max_{k_{1}\in[k-1]}{\cal R}_{1}^{2k_{1}}\left({\cal R}_{2}^{2(k-k_{1})}+{\cal R}_{3}^{2(k-k_{1})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{-2(k-1)}\left[{\cal R}_{2}^{2k}+{\cal R}_{3}^{2k}+{\cal R}_{1}^{2(k-1)}\left({\cal R}^{2}_{2}+{\cal R}^{2}_{3}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{2k}}{\lambda^{2(k-1)}}\bigg[\frac{K^{2k}}{\alpha^{2k}n^{2k}}\frac{\ell^{2k}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2k}}+\frac{K^{2k}[(d+n)\cdot h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})]^{k}}{\alpha^{2k}n^{2k}}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{dK}{\alpha n}\right)^{k-1}\left(\frac{K^{2}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}\frac{\ell^{2}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2}}+\frac{K^{2}(d+n)\cdot h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}\right)\bigg]

Combining the above fact, (
A.1
) and the upper bound
2{2k\choose k}\leq 2^{2k+1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}2^{2k+1}\cdot\frac{C^{k}r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\lambda^{-2(k-1)}\left[{\cal R}_{2}^{2k}+{\cal R}_{3}^{2k}+{\cal R}_{1}^{2(k-1)}\left({\cal R}^{2}_{2}+{\cal R}^{2}_{3}\right)\right]

(34)

The first term of (
A.1
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}(4C)^{2k}\cdot\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\lambda^{-2(k-1)}{\cal R}_{2}^{2k}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}C^{\prime 2k}\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\frac{K^{2k}}{\alpha^{2k}n^{2k}}\frac{h^{2(k-1)}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\kappa_{0}^{2(k-1)}\lambda^{2(k-1)}\ell^{2}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\lambda^{2(k-1)}\Delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\frac{K^{2}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}\frac{\ell^{2}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\frac{\alpha^{-1}Kr(dr/n+1)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

where we’ve used in (a) that the definition of
{\cal R}_{2}
, in (b) that
h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\lesssim\kappa_{0}^{-1}(\alpha n/K)
, in (c) that
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\Delta^{2}(\alpha n/{K})
, and in (d) that

\Delta^{2}\gg\alpha^{-1}K^{2}r\left({dr}/n+1\right)
.

The second term of (
A.1
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}(4C)^{2k}\cdot\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\lambda^{-2(k-1)}{\cal R}_{3}^{2k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}C^{\prime 2k}\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\frac{K^{2k}}{\alpha^{2k}n^{2k}}\frac{(d^{k}+n^{k})h^{k}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\lambda^{2(k-1)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}C^{\prime 2k}\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\frac{\kappa_{0}^{2(k-1)}r^{k-1}K^{2k}}{\alpha^{2k}n^{2k}}\frac{(d^{k}+n^{k})h^{k}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2(k-1)}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}C^{\prime 2k}\frac{r(dr+n)^{2}}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{4}}\frac{\kappa_{0}^{2(k-1)}K^{k}}{\alpha^{k}n^{k}}h^{k-1}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\frac{\alpha^{-1}Kr(dr/n+1)^{2}}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{4}}\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

where we’ve used in (a) that
\lambda^{2}\geq\kappa_{0}^{-2}r^{-1}\Delta^{2}
, in (b) that
h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\Delta^{2}\leq\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
and

\Delta^{2}\geq C\alpha^{-1}Kr(d/n+1)
, in (c) that
h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\lesssim\kappa_{0}^{-2}(\alpha n/K)
,
in (d) that
\Delta^{2}\gg\alpha^{-1/2}Kr^{1/2}\left({dr}/n+1\right)
.

The last term of (
A.1
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}(4C)^{2k}\cdot\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\lambda^{-2(k-1)}{\cal R}_{1}^{2(k-1)}\left({\cal R}^{2}_{2}+{\cal R}^{2}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\left({\cal R}^{2}_{2}+{\cal R}^{2}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\frac{r(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\frac{K^{2}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}\left(\frac{\ell^{2}_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2}}+\frac{(d+n)\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\left[\frac{\alpha^{-1}Kr(dr/n+1)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})+\frac{\alpha^{-2}K^{2}r(dr/n+1)^{2}}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{4}}\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}\frac{2}{4^{k+2}}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

where we’ve used in (a) that
\lambda^{2}\gtrsim\alpha^{-1}Kd/n
, in (b) that
h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\Delta^{2}\leq\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
, in (c) that
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\Delta^{2}(\alpha n/{K})
.

Collecting the above bounds and (
A.1
), we conclude that the second term on RHS of (
A.1
) can bounded as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{k+6}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{1}{4^{k+2}}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\frac{1}{32}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

Step 2.2.2: Treating the terms of
\big<\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\big)\big>

By symmetry, we can bound
\big<\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{M}_{a}\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\big)\big>
the same way as
\big<\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}\big>
, and the proof is omitted.

Step 2.2.3: Treating the terms of
\big<\mathbf{E}_{i},\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\big)\mathbf{M}_{a}\big(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\big)\big>

By Lemma
5
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}_{a}\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{32}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(35)

where we define
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}:={\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}\left(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\right)-{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
similar to
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}
.
We start by bounding the first term on RHS of (
35
). Using Lemma
5
, for any
k,l\geq 1
,
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
can be written as a sum of at most
{2k\choose k}^{2}
series, with all non-zero terms taking the form of

\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{4k-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}

where
\mathbf{U}\in\{\pm\mathbf{U}_{a},\pm\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}\}
and
\mathbf{V}\in\{\mathbf{V}_{a},\mathbf{V}_{a\perp}\}
, and
\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\left\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bigcup\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}})
for
j\in[4k-1]
. Moreover, we have
