\left|\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}})\}\right|=2k
and
\left|\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\}\right|=2k-1
. Notice that by setting
\tilde{k}=2k
, this reduces to the case when we treat
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},\tilde{k}}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}
. Following the same argument line by line (except for adjusting the constants accordingly), we can arrive at with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-\delta\left[\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]\right]^{1/2}\Delta\big)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\left(-\Delta^{2}\cdot\frac{c\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}{\alpha^{-1}Kr(d+\log n)/n}\right)

For the second and third terms on RHS of (
35
), using Lemma
6
we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a\in[K]}\sum_{b\in[K]\backslash\{a\}}\frac{C(dr+n)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}\left(\left\|\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\|^{2}+\left\|{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}\right\|^{2}\right)

By definition,
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
consists of at most
2\cdot 3^{k}{2k\choose k}
terms and
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}
consists of at most
2\cdot 3^{2k}{2k\choose k}
terms, each being in form of

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{4k-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}

where
\mathbf{U}\in\{\pm\mathbf{U}_{a},\pm\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}\}
,
\mathbf{V}\in\{\mathbf{V}_{a},\mathbf{V}_{a\perp}\}
and
\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\left\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}}\right)\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}}\right)
for
j\in[4k-1]
with
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\right|=2k-1
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{1}
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{2}
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{3}
and
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=2k
,
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\geq 0
,
k_{1}\leq 2k-1
. Again, this reduces to exact the case of
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},2k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}
. Following the same proof and adjusting constants therein, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{2^{2k+7}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{32}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

Step 2.2.4: Treating the terms of
\big<\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\Delta^{(t-1)}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\big>

The following decomposition is obvious:

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\Delta^{(t-1)}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)\right\rangle

(36)

The first term above, i.e.,
\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle
, is essentially the same as
\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}_{\mathbf{M},1}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\rangle
. For the second term of (
36
), we further have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

Note that

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})(\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right\rangle

Here,
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}
is of the same structure as
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k+1}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}
,
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})(\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}
are
of the same structure as
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k+1}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}
. By symmetry, the third term of (
36
) can be handled similarly. For the last term of (
36
), it can be decomposed as

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{a}\mathbf{U}_{a}^{\top}\right)(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)})\left(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{a}\mathbf{V}_{a}^{\top}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}){\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\rangle+\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}\right\rangle+\sum_{k,l\geq 1}\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})(\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}){\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\right\rangle

Notice that
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
is of the same structure as
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k+1}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
,
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k}^{(t-1)}\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
is of the same structure as
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},k+1}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}{\cal S}^{\mathbf{V}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},l}(\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a})
,
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}
is of the same structure as
{\cal S}^{\mathbf{U}_{a}}_{\mathbf{M},k+1}(\Delta^{(t-1)})\mathbf{M}_{a}\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{V}_{a},l}^{(t-1)}
. It suffices to note that the last term consists of at most
2\cdot 3^{2k}{2k\choose k}
terms, each being in form of

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{2}\cdots\mathbf{W}_{4k-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}

where
\mathbf{U}\in\{\pm\mathbf{U}_{a},\pm\mathbf{U}_{a\perp}\}
,
\mathbf{V}\in\{\mathbf{V}_{a},\mathbf{V}_{a\perp}\}
and
\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\left\{\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}}\right)\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\bigcup\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}}\right)
for
j\in[4k-1]
with
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\}\right|=2k-1
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{1}
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{2}
,
\left|\left\{j:\mathbf{W}_{j}\in\mathcal{M}\left({\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}}\right)\right\}\right|=k_{3}
and
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=2k
,
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\geq 0
,
k_{1}\leq 2k-1
. This again reduces to the case of
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathbf{U}_{a},2k}^{(t-1)}\mathbf{M}_{a}
.

So far we finish the analysis of
\beta_{1,2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
and by symmetry the term
\beta_{1,1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
can be handled in a similar way.

Step 2.3: Bounding
\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

Recall the definition of
{\cal R}(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})
, we have that

\displaystyle\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left({\cal R}(a;\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})\geq\frac{\delta}{4}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(37)

We need to bound three terms on RHS of eq. (
37
) separately. It follows from Lemma
4
that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq C\left(\frac{K^{2}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}\frac{\ell^{2}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2}}+\frac{K^{2}{(d+n)h_{s_{i}^{\ast}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}+{\frac{dK}{\alpha n}}\right)

Then for the first term on RHS of eq. (
37
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}\neq s_{i}^{\ast}\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\max_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\frac{\frac{K^{4}\ell^{4}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\alpha^{4}n^{4}\Delta^{4}}+\frac{K^{4}(d^{2}+n^{2})h^{2}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\alpha^{4}n^{4}}+{\frac{d^{2}K^{2}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}}}{\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\frac{C^{\prime}\max_{b\in[K]}{\left({\frac{K^{4}\ell^{4}_{b}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\alpha^{4}n^{4}\Delta^{4}}+\frac{K^{4}{(d^{2}+n^{2})\ell^{2}_{b}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha^{4}n^{4}\Delta^{4}}}+{\frac{d^{2}K^{2}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}}}\right)}}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

where in the last inequality we’ve used
\Delta^{2}\gg\tau{K}/({\alpha n})
,
\Delta^{2}\gg\alpha^{-1}K\left({d}/n+1\right)
and
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\tau
. Similarly, we can bound the second term on RHS of eq. (
37
) as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{1}{6}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

It remains to consider the last term on RHS of eq. (
37
), which has the following bound:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}\left(\frac{K\ell_{s_{i}^{\ast}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\alpha n\Delta}+\frac{K\sqrt{(d+n)h_{s_{i}^{\ast}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha n}+\sqrt{\frac{dK}{\alpha n}}\right)

Hence we can obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[K]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t)}=a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\cdot\frac{\max_{b\in[K]}\left({\frac{K^{2}\ell^{2}_{b}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}\Delta^{2}}+\frac{K^{2}{(d+n)\ell_{b}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha^{2}n^{2}\Delta^{2}}}+{\frac{dK}{\alpha n}}\right)}{\delta^{2}\Delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

provided that
\Delta^{2}\gg\tau{K}/({\alpha n})
,
\Delta^{2}\gg\alpha^{-1}K\left({d}/{n}+1\right)
and
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\tau
. Collecting the above facts, we conclude that

\displaystyle\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq\frac{1}{2}\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

Step 3: Obtaining contraction property

Collecting all pieces in the previous steps, we arrive at with probability at least
1-\exp(-\Delta)
:

\displaystyle\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq n\exp\left(-(1-o(1))\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\ell(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})+\frac{1}{2}\ell(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

as
\Delta^{2}/\left[Kr(d+\log n)(\alpha n)^{-1}\right]\to\infty
.
As a consequence, we obtain the contraction property (
21
).

To finish the proof for any
t\geq 1
, we use a mathematical induction step. At iteration
t=1
, the conclusion holds via above argument together with the initialization conditions (
10
) and (
42
). Now suppose at iteration
t-1
for
t\geq 2
,
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
satisfies (
10
) and
h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
satisfies (
42
), via above argument we can obtain
\ell(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq 2n\exp\left(-(1-o(1))\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8}\right)+\ell(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)})/2\leq\tau
as long as
\Delta^{2}\gg|\log(\tau/n)|
, which is automatically met by the condition for
\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
.
Moreover, we also have

h(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq\Delta^{-2}\ell(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq\frac{\tau}{\Delta^{2}}=o\left(\frac{\alpha n}{\kappa_{0}^{2}K}\right)

This implies the conditions
\ell(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq\tau
and
h(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq\kappa_{0}^{-2}\alpha n/8K
hold for all
t\geq 0
and hence (
21
) holds for all
t\geq 1
. Using the relation
h(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq\Delta^{-2}\ell(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
and the condition
\Delta^{2}\gg\kappa_{0}^{2}K\tau/(\alpha n)
, with probability greater than
1-\exp(-\Delta)
, for each
t\geq 0
we have that

n^{-1}\cdot h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s})\leq\exp\left(-(1-o(1))\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8}\right)+2^{-t}

The proof is completed by applying a union bound accounting for the events
{\cal Q}_{1},{\cal Q}_{2},{\cal Q}_{3},{\cal Q}_{4}
.

A.2
Proof of Theorem
2

We first characterize the error of
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}
and without loss of generality, we only consider
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}
. Following the same argument in the proof of Theorem 1 in
Zhang and Xia 2018
, one can obtain that there exists some absolute constant
c_{0},C_{0}>0
such that if
\sigma_{\min}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))\geq C_{0}(dr_{\mathbf{U}})^{1/2}n^{1/4}
, then with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{0}(n\wedge d))
:

\left\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{}\mathbf{U},\mathbf{U}^{\ast})\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\frac{C(dr_{\mathbf{U}})^{1/2}\left[\sigma_{\min}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))+(dn)^{1/2}\right]}{\sigma^{2}_{\min}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}

Combined with the bound for
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}
, we conclude that if
\max\{\sigma_{\min}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}})),\sigma_{\min}({\mathscr{M}}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))\}\geq C_{0}(dr_{\mathbf{U}})^{1/2}n^{1/4}
, then with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{0}(n\wedge d))
:

\max\left\{\left\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{}\mathbf{U},\mathbf{U}^{\ast})\right\|_{\rm F},\left\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{}\mathbf{V},\mathbf{V}^{\ast})\right\|_{\rm F}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}

(38)

Denote the above event by
{\cal Q}_{0,1}
and we proceed on
{\cal Q}_{0,1}
.

We then analyze the performance of spectral clustering based on
\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}={\bm{\mathcal{X}}}\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
. Our proof is based on the proof for Lemma 4.2 in
Löffler et al. 2021
with slight modification. Let
\bm{\mathcal{G}}:={\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
denote the signal part of
\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}
(also
\mathbf{G}:={\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
) and
{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}=[vec(\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{1}^{(0)}}),\cdots,vec(\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{n}^{(0)}})]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d_{1}d_{2}}
denote the corresponding k-means solution. We claim the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to Section
B
.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose
{\cal Q}_{0,1}
holds. Then we have the following facts:

(I)

{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}
, the k-means solution, is close to
\mathbf{G}
, i.e., there exists some absolute constants
c_{0},C_{0}>0
such that with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{0}d)
:

\left\|{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}-\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{K}\left(\sqrt{dKr+n}\right)

(II)

The rows of
\mathbf{G}
belonging to different clusters is well-separated, i.e.

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\mathcal{G}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}

for any
i,j\in[n],s_{i}^{\ast}\neq s_{j}^{\ast}
.

We proceed on the event
{\cal Q}_{0,2}:=\{\text{(I) holds}\}
.
Define the following set

S=\left\{i\in[n]:\left\|[{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}]_{i\cdot}-[\mathbf{G}]_{i\cdot}\right\|\geq\frac{\Delta}{4}\right\}

Then by construction we have

|S|\leq\frac{\left\|{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}-\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{(\Delta/4)^{2}}\leq\frac{\alpha n}{2K}

where the last inequality is due to the condition
\Delta^{2}\geq{32C_{0}^{2}\alpha^{-1}K^{2}}\left({dKr}/{n}+1\right)
.

We claim that all indices in
S^{c}
are correctly clustered. To see this, let

N_{k}=\{i\in[n]:s_{i}^{\ast}=k,i\in S^{c}\}

The following two facts hold:

•

For each
k\in[K]
,
|N_{k}|\geq n_{k}^{\ast}-|S|\geq\alpha n/(2K)>0

•

For each pair
a,b\in[K],a\neq b
, there cannot exist some
i\in N_{a}
and
j\in N_{b}
such that
\widehat{s}_{i}^{(0)}=\widehat{s}_{j}^{(0)}
. Otherwise we have
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(0)}}=\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{j}^{(0)}}
and it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|[\mathbf{G}]_{i\cdot}-[\mathbf{G}]_{j\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|[\mathbf{G}]_{i\cdot}-[{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}]_{i\cdot}\right\|+\left\|[{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}]_{i\cdot}-[{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}]_{j\cdot}\right\|+\left\|[{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}]_{j\cdot}-[\mathbf{G}]_{j\cdot}\right\|

\displaystyle<\frac{\Delta}{2}

which contradicts (II).

The above two facts imply that sets
\{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(0)}:i\in N_{k}\}
are disjoint for all
k\in[K]
. Therefore, there exists a permutation
\pi
such that
\sum_{i\in S^{c}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(0)}\neq\pi(s_{i}^{\ast})\right)=0
, i.e., indices in
S^{c}
are correctly clustered. Therefore, we have that

n^{-1}\cdot h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq n^{-1}\cdot|S|\leq\frac{C{K}}{\Delta^{2}}\left(\frac{dKr}{n}+1\right)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle n^{-1}\cdot\ell_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{i}^{(0)}}-\mathbf{M}_{\pi(s_{i}^{\ast})}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(0)}\neq\pi(s_{i}^{\ast})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}|S|\gamma^{2}\Delta^{2}\leq C{\gamma^{2}K}\left(\frac{dKr}{n}+1\right)

The proof is completed by taking union bound over
{\cal Q}_{0}^{c}:={\cal Q}_{0,1}^{c}\bigcup{\cal Q}_{0,2}^{c}
.

A.3
Proof of Theorem
3

We essentially follow a similar argument of
Gao et al. 2018
. Without loss of generality we assume
\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}=\Delta
. Consider the
\mathbf{s}^{\ast}\in[K]^{n}
such that
n_{1}^{\ast}\leq n_{2}^{\ast}\leq\cdots\leq n_{K}^{\ast}
and
n_{1}^{\ast}=n_{2}^{\ast}=\lfloor\alpha n/K\rfloor
. For every
k\in[K]
, we can choose a subset
\mathfrak{N}_{k}\subset\{i\in[n]:s_{i}^{*}=k\}
with cardinality
\lceil n_{k}^{\ast}-\frac{\alpha n}{4K^{2}}\rceil
. And let
\mathfrak{N}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}\mathfrak{N}_{k}
denote the collection of samples in
\mathfrak{N}_{k}
’s. Define the following parameter space for
\mathbf{s}
:

\mathbf{S}^{\ast}=\{\mathbf{s}\in[K]^{n}:s_{i}=s_{i}^{\ast}\text{~for~}i\in\mathfrak{N}\}

For any two
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}
such that
\mathbf{s}\neq\mathbf{s}^{\prime}
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(s_{i}\neq s_{i}^{\prime})\leq\frac{K}{n}\frac{\alpha n}{4K^{2}}=\frac{\alpha}{4K}

Meanwhile, for any permutation
\pi\neq\text{Id}
from
[K]
to
[K]
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(\pi(s_{i})\neq s_{i}^{\prime})\geq\frac{K}{n}\left(\frac{\alpha n}{K}-\frac{\alpha n}{4K^{2}}\right)\geq\frac{3\alpha}{4K}

Therefore, we conclude that
h_{\textsf{c}}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\prime})=h(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}(s_{i}\neq s_{i}^{\prime})
for any
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}
. Define the parameter space

\displaystyle\Omega(d_{1},d_{2},n,K,\alpha)=\Big\{(\{\mathbf{M}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K},\mathbf{s}):~

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}},\text{rank}(\mathbf{M}_{k})=r_{k},\forall k\in[K],\mathbf{s}\in[K]^{n},

\displaystyle\min_{k\in[K]}|\{i\in[n]:s_{i}=k\}|\geq\alpha n/K,\min_{a\neq b}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{b}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\Delta\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\Omega_{0}(d_{1},d_{2},n,K,\alpha)=\Big\{(\{\mathbf{M}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K},\mathbf{s}):~

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}},\text{rank}(\mathbf{M}_{k})=r_{k},\forall k\in[K],\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast},

\displaystyle\min_{k\in[K]}|\{i\in[n]:s_{i}=k\}|\geq\alpha n/K,\min_{a\neq b}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{b}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\Delta\Big\}

Since
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
, we have

\inf_{\widehat{}\mathbf{s}}\sup_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s})\geq\inf_{\widehat{}\mathbf{s}}\sup_{\Omega_{0}}\mathbb{E}h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s})\geq\inf_{\widehat{}\mathbf{s}}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}}\mathbb{E}h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s})\geq\sum_{i\in\mathfrak{N}^{c}}\inf_{\widehat{s}_{i}}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{i}\neq s_{i})

(39)

where we consider a uniform prior on
\mathbf{S}^{\ast}
and hence the second inequality holds as minimax risk is lower bounded by Bayes risk, and the last inequality holds since the infimum can be taken over all
\widehat{}\mathbf{s}
such that
\widehat{s}_{i}=s^{\ast}_{i}
for
i\in\mathfrak{N}
. Then it suffices to consider
\inf_{\widehat{s}_{i}}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{i}\neq s_{i})
for
i\in\mathfrak{N}^{c}
. Without loss generality, we assume
1\in\mathfrak{N}^{c}
and for any
k\in[K]
we denote
\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\ast}=\{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}:s_{1}=k\}
. It’s obvious that
\mathbf{S}^{\ast}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\ast}
and
\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{a}\bigcap\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{b}=\phi
for
a\neq b
. In addition, by the definition of such partition, for any
a\neq b\in[K]
and
\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{a}
, there exists a unique
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{b}
such that
s_{i}=s^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i\neq 1
, which implies that
|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{a}|=|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{b}|
for all
a,b\in[K]
. Then we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{s}_{1}}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq s_{1})

\displaystyle=\inf_{\widehat{s}_{1}}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}|}\frac{1}{K-1}\sum_{a<b}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{a}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq a)+\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{b}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq b)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K(K-1)}\sum_{a<b}\inf_{\widehat{s}_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{a}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{a}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq a)+\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{b}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{b}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq b)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K(K-1)}\inf_{\widehat{s}_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{|S_{1}^{*}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in S_{1}^{*}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq 1)+\frac{1}{|S_{2}^{*}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}\in S_{2}^{*}}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq 2)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K(K-1)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{-1}|}\sum_{\mathbf{s}_{-1}\in\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{-1}}\inf_{\widehat{s}_{1}}\left(\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{s}=(1,\mathbf{s}_{-1})}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq 1)+\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{s}=(2,\mathbf{s}_{-1})}(\widehat{s}_{1}\neq 2)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{K(K-1)}\inf_{\widehat{s}_{1}}\Big(\mathbb{P}_{H_{0}^{(1)}}(\widehat{s}_{1}=2)+\mathbb{P}_{H_{1}^{(1)}}(\widehat{s}_{1}=1)\Big)

(40)

where
\mathbf{S}^{\ast}_{-1}
is the collection of the subvectors in
\mathbf{S}^{\ast}
excluding the first coordinate, and we define a simple hypothesis testing for each
i\in[n]
:

H_{0}^{(i)}:s_{i}=1\text{~~~vs.~~~}H_{1}^{(i)}:s_{i}=2

Hence in (
A.3
), we have the form of Type-I error
+
Type-II error of the above test.
Notice that
|\{i\in[n]:s_{i}^{*}=k\}\backslash\mathfrak{N}_{k}|\geq\lfloor\alpha n/(4K^{2})\rfloor
and hence
|\mathfrak{N}^{c}|\geq c_{0}\alpha n/K
for some constant
c_{0}>0
. Combining this with (
39
), (
A.3
), we proceed that

\inf_{\widehat{}\mathbf{s}}\sup_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s})\geq c_{0}\frac{\alpha n}{K^{3}}\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{N}^{c}|}\sum_{i\in\mathfrak{N}^{c}}\inf_{\widehat{s}_{i}}\Big(\mathbb{P}_{H_{0}^{(l)}}(\widehat{s}_{i}=2)+\mathbb{P}_{H_{1}^{(l)}}(\widehat{s}_{i}=1)\Big)

According to the Neyman-Pearson lemma, for each
i\in[n]
, the optimal test of
H_{0}^{(l)}\text{~vs.~}H_{1}^{(l)}
is given by the likelihood ratio test with threshold
1
. Let
p_{0}(\mathbf{X}_{i})
and
p_{1}(\mathbf{X}_{i})
denote the likelihood of
\mathbf{X}_{i}
under
H_{0}
and
H_{1}
, respectively. Then
\frac{p_{1}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}{p_{0}(\mathbf{X}_{i})}=\frac{\exp(\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}/2)}{\exp(\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}/2)}
and hence the infimum is achieved by
\widehat{s}_{i}=\argmin_{k\in\{1,2\}}\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}-\mathbf{M}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{s}_{i}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}_{H_{0}^{(l)}}(\widehat{s}_{i}=2)+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}_{H_{1}^{(l)}}(\widehat{s}_{i}=1)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}+\mathbf{E}_{i}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}+\mathbf{E}_{i}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\langle\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1},\mathbf{E}_{i}\rangle\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\langle\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2},\mathbf{E}_{i}\rangle\right)\right)

Notice that
\langle\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1},\mathbf{E}_{i}\rangle\overset{\text{d}}{=}\langle\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2},\mathbf{E}_{i}\rangle\overset{\text{d}}{=}{\cal N}(0,\sigma^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2})
, we can proceed as

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{s}_{i}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}_{H_{0}^{(l)}}(\widehat{s}_{i}=2)+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}_{H_{1}^{(l)}}(\widehat{s}_{i}=1)\right)\geq\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}\right)

where the inequality holds as
\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}/\sigma\geq 1
. Hence we conclude that

\inf_{\widehat{}\mathbf{s}}\sup_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}n^{-1}\cdot h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s})\geq\exp\left(-\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}-C\log\frac{\Delta K}{\alpha\sigma}\right)=\exp\left(-(1+o(1))\frac{\Delta^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}\right)

provided that
\frac{\Delta^{2}}{\sigma^{2}\log(K/\alpha)}\rightarrow\infty
.

A.4
Proof of Theorem
5

Suppose we are given the data
\{\mathbf{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
generated by eq:rank-one-model with
((1-\epsilon)\mathbf{M},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}^{(n)}}
for any
\epsilon\in(0,1]
. We utilize the sample splitting trick, similar to that in Theorem 2.4 in
Löffler et al. 2020
, to generate two independent copies
\{\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(1)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\{\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(2)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
by

\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(1)}=\frac{\mathbf{X}_{i}+\epsilon^{-1}\widetilde{}\mathbf{E}_{i}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{-2}}},\quad\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(2)}=\frac{\mathbf{X}_{i}-\epsilon\widetilde{}\mathbf{E}_{i}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}

for
i=1,\cdots,n
where
\{\widetilde{}\mathbf{E}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are Gaussian noise matrices independent of
\{\mathbf{E}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. As a consequence, we have
\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(1)}=\frac{s_{i}^{\ast}\mathbf{M}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{-2}}}+\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(2)}=\frac{s_{i}^{\ast}\mathbf{M}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}+\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(2)}
with
\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(1)}=\frac{\mathbf{E}_{i}+\epsilon^{-1}\widetilde{}\mathbf{E}_{i}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{-2}}}
and
\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(2)}=\frac{\mathbf{E}_{i}-\epsilon\widetilde{}\mathbf{E}_{i}}{\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{2}}}
. Due to the property of Gaussian,
\{\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(1)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\{\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(2)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are independent. We define the following test statistic:

T_{n}=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{s}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(1)}}{n}\right\|

where
(\widehat{s}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{s}_{n})=\widehat{}\mathbf{s}_{\textsf{comp}}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(2)})
with
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{(2)}
being the data tensor by stacking
\left\{\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(2)}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
. By construction,
\{\widehat{s}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is independent of
\left\{\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(1)}\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
and hence
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{s}_{i}\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(1)}}{n}\overset{d}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(1)}}{n}
. Under
H_{0}
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-d)
:

T_{n}=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{s}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{(1)}}{n}\right\|\leq\frac{C_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}

for some absolute constant
C_{0}>0
. Under
H_{1}
, we have

((1+\epsilon^{2})^{-1/2}\mathbf{M},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}^{(n)}}
since
(1-\epsilon)\leq(1+\epsilon^{2})^{-1/2}
. By (
17
) we have that with probability greater than
1-\zeta_{n}
:

n^{-1}\cdot h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}_{\textsf{comp}},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\delta_{n}

(41)

Without loss of generality we assume
h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}_{\textsf{comp}},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}_{\textsf{comp}},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
. Hence we can obtain with probability at least
1-\zeta_{n}-\exp(-d)
:

\displaystyle T_{n}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{s}_{i}s_{i}^{\ast}}{n\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{-2}}}\mathbf{M}\right\|-\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{s}_{i}\mathbf{E}_{i}^{(1)}}{n}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}^{(n)}(1-2n^{-1}h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}_{\textsf{comp}},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}))}{\sqrt{n}\cdot\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{-2}}}-\frac{C_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}

\displaystyle>\frac{C_{0}}{2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}

where we’ve used (
41
) and
\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}^{(n)}>C_{0}(1-2\delta_{n})^{-1}\sqrt{1+\epsilon^{-2}}d^{1/2}
in the last inequality. Then the test
\phi_{n}
can be defined as

\displaystyle\phi_{n}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if~}T_{n}>C_{0}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}},\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

It turns out that

\mathbb{E}_{Q_{n}}[\phi_{n}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})]+\sup_{((1-\epsilon)\mathbf{M},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\in\widetilde{\Omega}_{\Lambda_{\textsf{\tiny min}}^{(n)}}}\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbf{M},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}[1-\phi_{n}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})]\leq\zeta_{n}+\exp(-d)

Notice that computing
T_{n}
requires only
poly(d,n)
and the proof is completed by setting
n,d\rightarrow\infty
.

A.5
Proof of Theorem
6

Theorem
7
can be obtained by modifying the proofs of Theorem
1
, and hence we only sketch the necessary modifications here. Similar to the proof of Theorem
1
, we have

\displaystyle h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\frac{\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta^{2}}=o\left(\frac{\alpha n}{\kappa_{0}^{2}}\right)

(42)

as a consequence of condition (
18
).

We consider the iterative convergence of Algorithm
3
. Following the same argument of Step 2 in the proof of Theorem
1
and adopting the same notation therein, we have the following inequality:

\displaystyle\ell(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\xi_{\textsf{err}}+\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})+\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

We can bound
\xi_{\textsf{err}}
the same as
Step 2.1
in the proof of Theorem
1
. To bound
\beta_{1}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
, it turns out that, by symmetry, we only need to bound

\displaystyle\beta_{1,2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 1\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 2\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(43)

The argument in
Step 2.2
in the proof of Theorem
1
can be directly applied to the analysis of
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{1}
, i.e., the first term on RHS of eq. (
A.5
), whereas it fails for
\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{2}
since
\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{M}_{2})

can
be arbitrarily close to
0
and Lemma
5
no longer holds. Observe that

\displaystyle\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{n_{2}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=2\right)\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{(t-1)}\right)\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\top}\left[\mathbf{M}_{2}+\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=2\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{2})+\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{2}+(\bar{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{(t-1)}-\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{2})\right]\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{M}_{2}+\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{2}+\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}+\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\right)\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\top}

where

\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}=\frac{1}{n_{2}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=2\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{2})\quad{\rm and}\quad\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}=\bar{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{(t-1)}-\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{2}

Notice that since
h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})
satisfies (
50
), we have
n_{2}^{(t-1)}\geq{7\alpha n}/{16}
.
Lemma
4
implies that under event
{\cal Q}_{1}\cap{\cal Q}_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq(1+c)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|+c\left(\alpha^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\alpha^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}\left(\alpha^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\alpha^{-1/2}\kappa_{0}^{-1}\right)

for some small universal constant
c^{\prime}>0
, where the second inequality is due to Assumption
2
. On the other hand, under event
{\cal Q}_{1}\cap{\cal Q}_{2}
and Assumption
2
we also have

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{1}^{(t)}\right\|\geq(1-c)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|-c^{\prime}\left(\alpha^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\alpha^{-1/2}\kappa_{0}^{-1}\right)>\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}\right\|

By taking a union bound over
{\cal Q}_{1}\cap{\cal Q}_{2}
, we conclude that with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
we have
\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}\right\|<\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{1}^{(t)}\right\|
and hence we set
{\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}}=0
afterwards. Then for the second term on RHS of eq. (
A.5
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{4}}{128\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-c\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}r_{1}}{\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|^{2}r_{2}}\delta^{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

where the last inequality is due to Assumption
. Hence the expecatation can be bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 2\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\leq n\Delta^{2}\exp\left[-c\delta^{2}r_{1}r_{2}^{-1}\left(\lambda_{1}/\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|\right)^{2}\Delta^{2}\right]

By Markov inequality, with probability at least
1-\exp\left[-\delta\left(\sqrt{r_{1}/r_{2}}\lambda_{1}/\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|\right)\Delta\right]
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 2\right)\cdot\mathbb{I}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\widehat{}\mathbf{M}^{(t)}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\delta}{8}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\exp\left(-\delta(\alpha n/K)^{1/2}\Delta^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n\cdot\exp\left[-\delta^{2}r_{1}r_{2}^{-1}\left(\lambda_{1}/\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|\right)^{2}\Delta^{2}\right]

which holds as long as
\delta\to 0
sufficiently slowly compared with
\lambda_{1}^{2}r_{1}r_{2}^{-1}/\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|^{2}\to\infty
.

It remains to consider
\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})
. Observe that

\displaystyle\beta_{2}(\mathbf{s}^{\ast},\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[2]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[2]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[2]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(44)

The first term on RHS of eq. (
44
) can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[2]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 1\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 2\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{1}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(45)

The second term of (
45
) can be bounded the same way as that in
Step 2.3
of the proof of Theorem
1
. Note that
\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq r_{2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|^{2}=o(r_{1}\lambda_{1}^{2})=o\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)
and hence the first term of (
45
) vanishes by setting
\delta
slowly converging to
0
. It suffices to consider the last term on RHS of eq. (
44
). Observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{a\in[2]\backslash\{s_{i}^{\ast}\}}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{a}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{a}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 1\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{1}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}^{(t)}_{i}\neq 2\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\mathbb{I}\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\delta}{12}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)

(46)

The first term of (
46
) can be bounded the same way as that in
Step 2.3
of the proof of Theorem
1
, and the second term vanishes as
\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}-\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{2}^{(t)}\right\|_{\rm F}=\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}=o\left(\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}\right)
.

By mimicing the remaining proofs of Theorem
1
, we can finish the proof of Theorem
6
.

A.6
Proof of Theorem
7

For notational simplicity, we denote the smallest non-trivial singular value of
\mathbf{M}_{1}
as
\lambda_{1}
.
Denote the following decomposition of tensor
{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}={\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}+{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2}
, where for
k\in[2]
, the
i
-th slice of
{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{k}
is defined as
[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{k}]_{\cdot\cdot i}=\mathbb{I}(\mathbf{s}_{i}^{\ast}=k)\mathbf{M}_{k}
. It turns out that
\mathbf{U}_{1}
is the leading-
r_{1}
left singular vectors of
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})
and
\mathbf{V}_{1}
is the leading-
r_{1}
left singular vectors of
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})
. We first show that
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}
are close to
\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\mathbf{V}_{1}
, respectively. Without loss of generality, we only consider
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}
. A key observation is that
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}
is also the leading-
r_{1}
left eigenvectors
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})
. Then write

\displaystyle{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}})

\displaystyle={\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle={\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})

\displaystyle+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})+\left[{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})\right]{\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})\left[{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})\right]^{\top}+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})

(47)

We are going to bound each term on RHS of eq. (
A.6
). The first term
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})
is the signal part and we have

\sigma_{\min}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}))=\sigma_{r_{1}}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}))\geq n_{1}^{\ast}\lambda_{1}^{2}

For the
2
nd,
3
rd and
4
th term of (
A.6
), we can have

\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})\right\|\leq 2\kappa_{0}\sqrt{n_{1}^{\ast}n_{2}^{\ast}}\lambda_{1}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|

and

\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})\right\|\leq n_{2}^{\ast}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|^{2}

The
5
th and
6
th term of eq. (
A.6
) can be together bounded as

\displaystyle\left\|\left[{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})\right]{\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})\left[{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1})+{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2})\right]^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\kappa_{0}\sqrt{n_{1}^{\ast}}\lambda_{1}+\sqrt{n_{2}^{\ast}}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|\right)\sqrt{d}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
, for some absolute constant
c,C>0
.
Lastly, we notice that
\mathbb{E}\left({\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})\right)=nd_{2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}}
, then by
Koltchinskii and Lounici 2017
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-d)
we have

\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(\bm{\mathcal{E}}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})-nd_{2}\mathbf{I}_{d_{1}}\right\|\leq C\sqrt{n}d

Note that
n_{2}^{\ast}/n_{1}^{\ast}\leq 2(1-\alpha/2)/\alpha\leq 2\alpha^{-1}
. Collecting all pieces above, if

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}\geq C\left(\kappa_{0}\alpha^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}+\alpha^{-1/2}\frac{d^{1/2}}{n^{1/4}}\right),\quad\lambda_{1}\geq\kappa_{0}\alpha^{-1/2}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|

(48)

for some large constant
C>0
. Note that
\kappa_{0}\alpha^{-1/2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}
in the first condition in (
48
) is trivial as we assume
n/\kappa_{0}^{4}\geq C
for some large constant
C>0
, and the second term is implied by the condition on
\sigma_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{M}_{1})
together with the assumption

\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|\leq C\kappa_{0}^{-1}\frac{d^{1/2}}{n^{1/4}}

Then with probability greater than
1-\exp(-cd)
we can have

\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\right\|\leq 1/4
. Using same analysis on
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}
, we can conclude with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
:

\max\left\{\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\right\|,\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\right\|\right\}\leq\frac{1}{6}

(49)

Define
\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}={\bm{\mathcal{X}}}\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}
,
\bm{\mathcal{G}}:={\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}
(also
\mathbf{G}:={\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})
) and
{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}:=[vec(\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{1}^{(0)}}),\cdots,vec(\widehat{}\mathbf{M}_{\widehat{s}_{n}^{(0)}})]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d_{1}d_{2}}
. We can have the following lemma, which is an analogue to Lemma
7
.

Lemma 8
.

Suppose (
49
) holds. Then we have the following facts:

(I)

{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}
, the k-means solution, is close
\mathbf{G}
, i.e., there exists some absolute constants
c_{0},C_{0}>0
such that with probability at least
1-\exp(-c_{0}d)
:

\left\|{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}-\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq C_{0}\left(\sqrt{dr_{1}+n}\right)

(II)

The rows of
\mathbf{G}
belonging to different clusters is well-separated, i.e.

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\mathcal{G}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}

for any
i,j\in[n],s_{i}^{\ast}\neq s_{j}^{\ast}
.

Following the almost identical argument in the proof of Theorem
2
but replacing
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}
with
\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}
and
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}
with
\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
we have

n^{-1}\cdot h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\frac{C}{\Delta^{2}}\left(\frac{dr_{1}}{n}+1\right)=o\left(\frac{\alpha}{\kappa_{0}^{2}}\right)

(50)

where the last equality holds provided that
\Delta^{2}\gg\kappa_{0}^{2}\alpha^{-1}\left({dr_{1}}/{n}+1\right)
. Since the condition in Theorem
6
already implies that

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}\gtrsim r_{1}\lambda_{1}^{2}\geq C\alpha^{-1}\frac{{dr_{1}}}{\sqrt{n}}

Then if
n/\kappa_{0}^{4}\rightarrow\infty
and
\alpha\Delta^{2}/\kappa_{0}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
, the condition
\Delta^{2}\gg\kappa_{0}^{2}\alpha^{-1}\left({dr_{1}}/{n}+1\right)
automatically holds.

As a result, we also have the following holds with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
:

\displaystyle\ell_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\Delta^{2}h_{\textsf{c}}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(0)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=o\left(\frac{\alpha n\Delta^{2}}{\kappa_{0}^{2}}\right)

which is an analogue to (
42
), where we’ve used
\gamma=1
in the two component case.

Appendix B
Proof of Technical Lemmas

B.1
Proof of Lemma
1

Without loss of generality we only proof
j=1
. It follows that

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{\min}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}^{-2}\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}})\right\|^{2}\geq\kappa_{1}^{-2}r_{\mathbf{U}}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{k}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{1}^{-2}r_{\mathbf{U}}^{-1}n\lambda^{2}\geq\kappa_{1}^{-2}(Kr)^{-1}n\lambda^{2}

where the last inequality is due to
r_{\mathbf{U}}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}\leq Kr
.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
2

By definition we have that

\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{I}_{r_{1}}&\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{2}&\cdots&\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{K}\\
\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{1}&\mathbf{I}_{r_{2}}&\cdots&\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{K}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mathbf{U}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{1}&\mathbf{U}_{K}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{2}&\cdots&\mathbf{I}_{r_{K}}\\
\end{bmatrix}

and
\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{W}=\text{diag}(n_{1}^{\ast},\cdots,n_{K}^{\ast})
. Hence we have

\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\otimes\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}n_{1}^{\ast}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}&\mathbf{0}&\cdots&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&n_{2}^{\ast}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}&\cdots&\mathbf{0}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\cdots&n_{K}^{\ast}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{U}\\
\end{bmatrix}

Simple calculations give that

\displaystyle{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}^{\top}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{U}{\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{S}}})(\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\otimes\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{V}){\mathscr{M}}_{1}^{\top}({\bm{\mathcal{S}}})\mathbf{U}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{U}\cdot\text{diag}(n_{1}^{\ast}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{1}^{2},\cdots,n_{K}^{\ast}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{K}^{2})\cdot\mathbf{U}^{\top}

As a result, we obtain

\sigma_{1}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}^{\top}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))\leq\sigma_{1}^{2}(\mathbf{U})\cdot\max_{1\leq k\leq K}{n_{k}^{\ast}\sigma^{2}_{\textsf{\tiny max}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{k})}

\sigma_{r_{\mathbf{U}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}^{\top}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))\geq\sigma_{r_{\mathbf{U}}}^{2}(\mathbf{U})\cdot\min_{1\leq k\leq K}{n_{k}^{\ast}\sigma^{2}_{\textsf{\tiny min}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{k})}

Hence we conclude that

\kappa_{1}=\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}^{\top}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))}{\sigma_{r_{\mathbf{U}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}^{\top}_{1}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}))}}\leq\kappa_{0}\kappa(\mathbf{U})\cdot\sqrt{\frac{n_{\textsf{\tiny max}}^{\ast}}{n_{\textsf{\tiny min}}^{\ast}}}

Similarly we can prove that
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}){\mathscr{M}}^{\top}_{2}({\bm{\mathcal{M}}})=\mathbf{V}\cdot\text{diag}(n_{1}^{\ast}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{1}^{2},\cdots,n_{K}^{\ast}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{K}^{2})\cdot\mathbf{V}^{\top}
and
\kappa_{1}\leq\kappa_{0}\kappa(\mathbf{U})\cdot({n_{\textsf{\tiny max}}^{\ast}}/{n_{\textsf{\tiny min}}^{\ast}})^{1/2}
.

If
r_{\mathbf{U}}=r_{\mathbf{V}}=r_{1}
, by min-max principle for singular values we have

\sigma_{\textsf{\tiny min}}(\mathbf{U})=\sigma_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{U})=\max_{S\subset\mathbb{R}^{n},\text{dim}(S)=r_{1}}\min_{x\in S,\left\|x\right\|=1}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}x\\
\vdots\\
\mathbf{U}_{K}^{\top}x\\
\end{bmatrix}\right\|\geq\max_{S\subset\mathbb{R}^{n},\text{dim}(S)=r_{1}}\min_{x\in S,\left\|x\right\|=1}\left\|\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}x\right\|=\sigma_{\textsf{\tiny min}}(\mathbf{U}_{1})=1

and

\sigma_{\textsf{\tiny max}}(\mathbf{U})=\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\left\|x\right\|=1}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}x\\
\vdots\\
\mathbf{U}_{K}^{\top}x\\
\end{bmatrix}\right\|\leq\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\left\|x\right\|=1}\left\|\mathbf{U}_{k}^{\top}x\right\|}=\sqrt{K}

Therefore, we have
\kappa(\mathbf{U})\leq K^{1/2}
and similarly
\kappa(\mathbf{V})\leq K^{1/2}
, from which we can conclude that
\max\{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\}\leq\kappa_{0}(K^{2}/\alpha)^{1/2}
.

If
r_{\mathbf{U}}=r_{\mathbf{V}}=\mathring{r}
and
\mathbf{U}_{k}
’s are mutually orthogonal, then
\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V}
has orthonormal columns and
\kappa(\mathbf{U})=\kappa(\mathbf{V})=1
. Hence we have
\max\{\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\}\leq\kappa_{0}(K/\alpha)^{1/2}
.

B.3
Proof of Lemma
3

Note that for fixed
k\in[K]
, we have
\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=k\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=k\right)}}
has i.i.d. sub-gaussian entries with mean zero and variance
(n_{k}^{\ast})^{-1}
. By random matrix theory there exists some absolute constants
c,C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=k\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=k\right)}}\right\|\geq C\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{\ast}_{k}}}\right)\leq\exp(-cd)

Applying a union bound over
[K]
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Q_{1}^{c})=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}\left\{\left\|\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=k\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=k\right)}}\right\|\geq C\sqrt{\frac{d}{n^{\ast}_{k}}}\right\}\right)\leq K\exp(-cd)\leq\exp(-c_{0}d)

for some absolute constant
c_{0}>0
, provided that
d\gtrsim\log K
. To prove the tail bound for
Q_{2}
, consider fixed set
I\subseteq[n]
, we have for any
t>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{|I|}}\sum_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\leq C\left(\sqrt{d}+t\right)\right)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2})

Applying a union bound over all subsets of
[n]
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Q_{2}^{c})=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{I\subseteq[n]}\left\{\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{|I|}}\sum_{i\in I}\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\leq C\left(\sqrt{d}+t\right)\right\}\right)\leq 2\exp(-t^{2}+n)

By choosing
t=C_{1}\left(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{d}\right)
for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
, we obtain the desired result. It suffices to prove the bound for
{\cal Q}_{3}
. Fix
i\in[n]
, then for any
t>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\frac{\sum_{j\neq i}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{j}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)}}\right\|\geq C\sqrt{\frac{d+t^{2}}{n^{\ast}_{a}}}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\geq C\sqrt{d+t^{2}}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)

Applying a union bound over
[n]
and
[K]
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{a=1}^{K}\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\left\|\frac{\sum_{j\neq i}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{j}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(s_{j}^{\ast}=a\right)}}\right\}\right\|\geq C\sqrt{\frac{d+t^{2}}{n^{\ast}_{a}}}\right)\leq 2nK\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\left\|\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|\geq C\sqrt{d+t^{2}}\right\}\right)\leq 2n\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)

We can take
t=C_{2}\sqrt{d}+\log n
for some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
(using
d\gtrsim\log K
) and the proof is completed.

B.4
Proof of Lemma
4

By definition,
\left\|\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}\right\|
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{a})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a,s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{a})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8K}{7\alpha n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a,s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8K}{7\alpha n}\cdot\frac{\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta}

An alternative bound for
\left\|\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}\right\|
:

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{a})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a,s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a\right)(\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}}-\mathbf{M}_{a})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8K}{7\alpha n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a,s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a\right)\left\|\mathbf{M}_{s_{i}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{M}_{a}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\kappa_{0}K}{7\alpha n}\cdot\lambda\cdot h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

where we’ve used
h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\leq\sum_{a\in[K]}h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})=h(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})

and the condition (
10
).
In other words, we have the following bound for
\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}
that will be utilized repeatedly later:

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\mathbf{M}}^{(t-1)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16K}{7\alpha n}\cdot\min\left\{\kappa_{0}\lambda h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast}),\frac{\ell_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}{\Delta}\right\}

(51)

Moreover, under
{\cal Q}_{1}
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\mathbf{E}}^{\ast}_{a}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d}{n_{a}^{\ast}}}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{dK}{\alpha n}}

and it remains to bound
\left\|\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\right\|
. Note that

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{\mathbf{E}}^{(t-1)}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{a}^{\ast}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)-\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\right]\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|+\left\|\frac{n_{a}^{\ast}-n_{a}^{(t-1)}}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}n_{a}^{\ast}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a,s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|+\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}\neq a,s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\cdot\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a,\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}\neq a\right)\right|\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{\ast}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n_{a}^{(t-1)}}\cdot\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}\neq a,\widehat{s}_{i}^{(t-1)}=a\right)\right|\left\|\frac{1}{n_{a}^{\ast}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=a\right)\mathbf{E}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\lesssim}\frac{K\sqrt{(d+n)h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha n}+\frac{K}{n}h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\sqrt{\frac{dK}{\alpha n}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\lesssim}\frac{K\sqrt{(d+n)h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})}}{\alpha n}

where in (a) we’ve used the fact that
{\cal Q}_{2}
holds and (b) is due to that fact that
h_{a}(\widehat{}\mathbf{s}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{s}^{\ast})\lesssim\alpha n/K
.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
5

The conclusion directly follows from dilation, i.e., define

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}^{*}:=\begin{bmatrix}\bf 0&\mathbf{X}\\
\mathbf{X}^{\top}&\bf 0\\
\end{bmatrix},\quad\mathbf{M}^{*}:=\begin{bmatrix}\bf 0&\mathbf{M}\\
\mathbf{M}^{\top}&\bf 0\\
\end{bmatrix},\quad\Delta^{*}:=\begin{bmatrix}\bf 0&\Delta\\
\Delta^{\top}&\bf 0\\
\end{bmatrix}

and applying Theorem 1 in
Xia 2021
.

B.6
Proof of Lemma
6

To decouple the potential dependency of
\mathbf{E}_{i}
and
\mathbf{\Xi}
, we employ the technical tool in
Mendelson 2016
, for which we need to introduce additional notations. Let
{\cal F}\subset L_{2}
be a class of function defined on some measure
\mu
. Denote
\mathbb{E}\left\|G\right\|_{\cal F}:=\mathbb{E}\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}G_{f}
where
\{G_{f}:f\in{\cal F}\}
is the centered canonical gaussian process indexed by
{\cal F}
. A class
{\cal F}
is
L
-subgaussian if for every
f,h\in{\cal F}\cup\{0\}
,
\left\|f-h\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq L\left\|f-h\right\|_{L_{2}}
. Here
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\psi_{2}}
is the standard
\psi_{2}
norm (sub-Gaussian norm). The following lemma is adapted from
Mendelson 2016
.

Lemma 9

(Theorem 1.13 in
Mendelson 2016
)
.

Let
{\cal F}
be a
L
-subgaussian class. There exists an absolute constant
c_{0}
and for every
q>4
there exists a constant
c_{1}(q)
that depends only on
q
for which the following holds. Let
{\cal F}
be a class of functions on
(\Omega,\mu)
, set
u\geq\max\{8,\sqrt{q}\}
and consider an integer
s_{0}\geq 0
. Then, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-c_{0}2^{s_{0}}u^{2})
, for every
f\in{\cal F}
,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f^{2}(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}f^{2})\right|\leq c_{1}(q)\left(u^{2}\tilde{\Lambda}^{2}_{s_{0},u}({\cal F})+u\sqrt{n}\left(d_{q}({\cal F})\tilde{\Lambda}_{s_{0},u}({\cal F})\right)\right)

where
\tilde{\Lambda}_{s_{0},u}({\cal F})
(see a formal definition in
Mendelson 2016
) can be further bounded by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Lambda}_{s_{0},u}({\cal F})\leq c_{2}L\left(\mathbb{E}\left\|G\right\|_{{\cal F}}+2^{s_{0}/2}d_{q}({\cal F})\right)

and
d_{p}({\cal F}):=\sup_{f\in{\cal F}}\left\|f\right\|_{L_{p}}
for any
p>0
.

In our case, denote
\mu
as the distribution of each
\mathbf{E}_{i}
. Define

{\cal X}_{r}=\left\{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}},\text{rank}(\mathbf{X})\leq r,\left\|\mathbf{X}\right\|\leq 1\right\}

and

{\cal F}_{r}:=\left\{f:f(\cdot)=\left\langle\cdot,\mathbf{X}\right\rangle,\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}\right\}
on
\mu
. Observe that for any
f,g\in{\cal F}_{r}
and any
\mathbf{E}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}\sim\mu
having the same distribution as
\mathbf{E}_{i}
,

\displaystyle\left\|f(\mathbf{E})-g(\mathbf{E})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}=\left\|\left\langle\mathbf{E},\mathbf{X}_{1}-\mathbf{X}_{2}\right\rangle\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\left\|\mathbf{X}_{1}-\mathbf{X}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}=\left\|\left\langle\mathbf{E},\mathbf{X}_{1}-\mathbf{X}_{2}\right\rangle\right\|_{L_{2}}

This indicates that
{\cal F}_{r}
is
L
-subgaussian class with
L\leq C
for some absolute constant
C>0
. Also notice that for any fixed
q\geq 2

\displaystyle d_{q}({\cal F}_{r})=\sup_{f\in{\cal F}_{r}}\left\|f\right\|_{L_{q}}=\sup_{\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}}\left\|\left\langle\mathbf{E},\mathbf{X}\right\rangle\right\|_{L_{q}}\lesssim\sup_{\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}}\left\|\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq\sqrt{r}

where we’ve used the the moment characterization of the
\psi_{2}
norm, and that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|G\right\|_{{\cal F}_{r}}=\mathbb{E}\sup_{f\in{\cal F}_{r}}G_{f}=\mathbb{E}\sup_{\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}}|\left\langle\mathbf{Z},\mathbf{X}\right\rangle|\leq r\mathbb{E}\left\|\mathbf{Z}\right\|\lesssim r\sqrt{d}

where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}}
has i.i.d. standard normal entries.
As a result, by choosing
s_{0}
such that
2^{s_{0}}\asymp d
,
q=5
,
u=8
, we can apply Lemma
9
and obtain that with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}}\sum_{i:s_{i}^{\ast}=b}

\displaystyle\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{X}\right\rangle^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)=\sup_{f\in{\cal F}_{r}}\sum_{i:s_{i}^{\ast}=b}\left(f^{2}(\mathbf{E}_{i})-\mathbb{E}f^{2}(\mathbf{E}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim r\left(dr+\sqrt{dr\cdot n_{b}^{\ast}}\right)

Hence with the same probability,

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{\Xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}},{\rm rank}(\mathbf{\Xi})\leq r\\
\|\mathbf{\Xi}\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{I}\left(s_{i}^{\ast}=b\right)\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{\Xi}\right\rangle^{2}=\sup_{\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}}\sum_{i:s_{i}^{\ast}=b}\left(\left\langle\mathbf{E}_{i},\mathbf{X}\right\rangle^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\right)+n_{b}^{\ast}\sup_{\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}}\left\|\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim r\left(dr+\sqrt{dr\cdot n_{b}^{\ast}}+n_{b}^{\ast}\right)\lesssim r\left(dr+n_{b}^{\ast}\right)

by noticing that
\left\|\mathbf{X}\right\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq r
for any
\mathbf{X}\in{\cal X}_{r}
.

B.7
Proof of Lemma
7

We first prove (I). By definition of k-means

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}-\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}-\widehat{}\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}+\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 2\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 2\sqrt{2K}\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}\right\|

It suffices to notice that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{}\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{G}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}({\bm{\mathcal{X}}}\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top})\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\otimes\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\right\|=\left\|{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(\bm{\mathcal{E}})(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\otimes\widehat{}\mathbf{U})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sqrt{d(r_{\mathbf{U}}+r_{\mathbf{V}})}+\sqrt{n}\right)

where the last inequality holds with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
by Lemma 5 in
Zhang and Xia 2018
. Hence there exists some
C_{0}>0
, and with probability at least
1-\exp(-cd)
we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}-\mathbf{G}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sqrt{K}\left(\sqrt{dKr+n}\right)

for some absolute constant
C_{0}>0
.

It remains to prove (II). By definition of
\bm{\mathcal{G}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\mathcal{G}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}-\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top})\times_{2}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle-\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\times_{2}(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}\mathbf{V}^{\top})\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\geq\Delta-\frac{\Delta}{4}-\frac{\Delta}{4}\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}

where we’ve used the fact that
{\cal Q}_{0}
holds and the equivalence between
\sqrt{2}\left\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{U}_{2})\right\|_{\rm F}
and projection distance
\left\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{2}\mathbf{U}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}
.

B.8
Proof of Lemma
8

The proof of (I) is identical to that in the proof of Lemma
7
and hence we only show (II). By definition of
\bm{\mathcal{G}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\mathcal{G}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle-\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}(\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top})\times_{2}\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle-\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}(\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top})\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle-\left\|\left[{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{2}\times_{3}(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top})\right]\times_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{U}_{1}^{\top}\times_{2}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}\widehat{}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}-\frac{\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}}{6}-\frac{\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}}{6}-\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}\frac{5}{9}\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq\frac{\Delta}{2}

where in (a) we’ve used (
49
), (b) and (c) are due to the facts that
\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}\geq 9\left\|\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}
and
\Delta=\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}-\mathbf{M}_{2}\right\|_{\rm F}\leq 10/9\cdot\left\|\mathbf{M}_{1}\right\|_{\rm F}
, by properly choosing the absolute constant
C
in Assumption
and the proof is therefore completed.
